\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}e^{-\frac{u^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}n}}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon^{1/6}\sqrt{n}}\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}\sqrt{2\pi m}}\frac{t}{\sqrt{k}}e^{-\frac{n}{2\sigma^{2}mk}(t-\frac{ku}{n})^{2}}dt.

By a change of variable
t\sqrt{\frac{n}{\sigma^{2}mk}}=z
, it follows that

\displaystyle H(u)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}e^{-\frac{u^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}n}}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon^{1/6}n/\sqrt{\sigma^{2}mk}}\frac{z}{\sigma^{2}\sqrt{2\pi n}}\sqrt{\frac{\sigma^{2}mk}{n}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}(z-\sqrt{\frac{k}{\sigma^{2}nm}}u)^{2}}dz

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}e^{-\frac{u^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}n}}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon^{1/6}n/\sqrt{\sigma^{2}mk}}\frac{z}{\sigma^{2}\sqrt{2\pi n}}\sqrt{\frac{\sigma^{2}mk}{n}}dz

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{c\varepsilon^{1/3}}{\sqrt{k}}\frac{n}{\sqrt{mk}}e^{-\frac{u^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}n}}\leqslant\frac{c\varepsilon^{1/12}}{\sqrt{k}}\phi\left(\frac{u}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right),

which, together with (
3.5
) and (
3.5
), implies that

\displaystyle J(u)

\displaystyle\leqslant c\varepsilon^{1/12}\frac{V(x)}{n}\phi\left(\frac{u}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)+c_{\varepsilon}\left(\alpha_{n}+n^{-\varepsilon}\right)\frac{V(x)}{n}.

Since
K_{11}=\int_{\mathbb{R}}g(u)J(u)du
, we obtain

(3.29)

\displaystyle K_{11}\leqslant c\varepsilon^{1/12}\frac{V(x)}{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}}g(t)\phi\left(\frac{t}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)dt+c_{\varepsilon}\left(\alpha_{n}+n^{-\varepsilon}\right)\frac{V(x)}{n}\|g\|_{1}.

We proceed to give an upper bound for
K_{2}
, which can be rewritten as

(3.30)

\displaystyle K_{2}=\int_{\mathbb{R}}L(t)\mathbb{P}\left(x+S_{k}\in dt,\tau_{x}>k\right),

where, for
t\in\mathbb{R}
,

(3.31)

\displaystyle L(t):=\mathds{1}_{\{t>\varepsilon^{1/6}\sqrt{n}\}}\mathbb{E}(f(t+S_{m});\tau_{t}\leqslant m).

The function
t\mapsto L(t)
is integrable on
\mathbb{R}
since
f
is integrable on
\mathbb{R}
.
Denote

(3.32)

\displaystyle M(t):=\mathds{1}_{\{t+\varepsilon>\varepsilon^{1/6}\sqrt{n}\}}\mathbb{E}\left(g(t+S_{m});\tau_{t-\varepsilon}\leqslant m\right),\quad t\in\mathbb{R}.

Then
L\leqslant_{\varepsilon}M
since
f\leqslant_{\varepsilon}g
.
Using the upper bound (
3.1
) of Theorem
3.1

and the fact that
\left\|M\right\|_{1}\leqslant\left\|g\right\|_{1}
, we obtain that
uniformly in
x\in[0,\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}]
,

\displaystyle K_{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant(1+c\varepsilon)\frac{2V(x)}{\sqrt{2\pi}\sigma^{2}k}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}M\left(t\right)\phi^{+}\left(\frac{t}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\right)dt

(3.33)

\displaystyle\quad+c\varepsilon^{1/4}\frac{V(x)}{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}}M(t)e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}\varepsilon^{1/2}n}}dt+c_{\varepsilon}\left(\alpha_{n}+n^{-\varepsilon}\right)\frac{V(x)}{n}\left\|g\right\|_{1}.

For the first term, in view of (
3.32
), we use the duality formula (Lemma
3.2
)
and the fact that the function
\phi^{+}
is bounded on
\mathbb{R}
to derive that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}M\left(t\right)\phi^{+}\left(\frac{t}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\right)dt

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\mathbb{E}\left(g(t+S_{m});\tau_{t-\varepsilon}\leqslant m\right)\mathds{1}_{\{t+\varepsilon>\varepsilon^{1/6}\sqrt{n}\}}\phi^{+}\left(\frac{t}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\right)dt

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\mathbb{E}\left(g(t+\varepsilon+S_{m});\tau_{t}\leqslant m\right)\mathds{1}_{\{t+2\varepsilon>\varepsilon^{1/6}\sqrt{n}\}}\phi^{+}\left(\frac{t+\varepsilon}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\right)dt

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}g(t+\varepsilon)\mathbb{E}\left[\phi^{+}\left(\frac{t+S_{m}^{*}+\varepsilon}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\right);t+S_{m}^{*}+2\varepsilon>\varepsilon^{1/6}\sqrt{n},\tau_{t}^{*}\leqslant m\right]dt

\displaystyle\leqslant c\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}g(t+\varepsilon)\mathbb{P}\left(t+S_{m}^{*}>\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon^{1/6}\sqrt{n},\tau_{t}^{*}\leqslant m\right)dt=:c(J_{1}+J_{2}),

where

(3.34)

\displaystyle J_{1}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\varepsilon^{1/4}\sqrt{n}}g(t+\varepsilon)\mathbb{P}\left(t+S_{m}^{*}>\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon^{1/6}\sqrt{n},\tau_{t}^{*}\leqslant m\right)dt,

(3.35)

\displaystyle J_{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{\varepsilon^{1/4}\sqrt{n}}^{\infty}g(t+\varepsilon)\mathbb{P}\left(t+S_{m}^{*}>\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon^{1/6}\sqrt{n},\tau_{t}^{*}\leqslant m\right)dt.

For
J_{1}
, observe that on the event
\{\tau_{t}^{*}\leqslant m\}
,
the inequality
t+S_{m}^{*}>\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon^{1/6}\sqrt{n}
implies that there exists
0\leqslant j\leqslant m

such that
S_{m}^{*}-S_{j}^{*}>\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon^{1/6}\sqrt{n}
.
Since
S_{j}^{*}=S_{m-j}-S_{m}
, it follows that
-S_{m-j}>\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon^{1/6}\sqrt{n}
for some
0\leqslant j\leqslant m-1

and hence

(3.36)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(t+S_{m}^{*}>\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon^{1/6}\sqrt{n},\tau_{t}^{*}\leqslant m\right)\leqslant\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{1\leqslant j\leqslant m}|S_{j}|\geqslant\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon^{1/6}\sqrt{n},\tau_{t}^{*}\leqslant m\right).

Noting that
m=[\varepsilon^{1/2}n]
,
we use the Fuk-Nagaev inequality (Lemma
3.4
)
with
n
replaced by
m
and
u=v=\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon^{1/6}\sqrt{n}
, and condition
2
to get

(3.37)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{1\leqslant j\leqslant m}|S_{j}|\geqslant\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon^{1/6}\sqrt{n}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant c\varepsilon^{1/6}+m\mathbb{P}(|X_{1}|>v)\leqslant c\varepsilon^{1/6}+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{2\varepsilon}},

which, together with (
3.34
) and (
3.36
), implies that

(3.38)

\displaystyle J_{1}\leqslant c\varepsilon^{1/6}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon^{1/4}\sqrt{n}}g(t+\varepsilon)dt+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{2\varepsilon}}\|g\|_{1}.

For
J_{2}
, we use (
3.36
), (
3.37
) and Hölder’s inequality to get

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(t+S_{m}^{*}>\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon^{1/6}\sqrt{n},\tau_{t}^{*}\leqslant m\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\mathbb{P}^{1/2}\left(\max_{1\leqslant j\leqslant m}|S_{j}|\geqslant\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon^{1/6}\sqrt{n}\right)\mathbb{P}^{1/2}\left(\tau_{t}^{*}\leqslant m\right)

(3.39)

\displaystyle\leqslant\left(c\varepsilon^{1/12}+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon}}\right)\mathbb{P}^{1/2}\left(t+\min_{1\leqslant j\leqslant m}S_{j}<0\right).

Denote

(3.40)

\displaystyle A_{m}=\left\{\sup_{0\leqslant t\leqslant 1}\left|S_{[tm]}-\sigma B_{tm}\right|\leqslant m^{1/2-2\varepsilon}\right\}

and by
A_{m}^{c}
its complement.
By the functional central limit theorem (Lemma
3.6
)
and the identity (
3.8
) of Lemma
3.5
, we obtain that for any
t\geqslant\varepsilon^{1/4}\sqrt{n}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(t+\min_{1\leqslant j\leqslant m}S_{j}<0\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\mathbb{P}\left(t+\min_{1\leqslant j\leqslant m}S_{j}<0,A_{m}\right)+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{2\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\mathbb{P}\left(t-m^{1/2-2\varepsilon}+\sigma\min_{1\leqslant j\leqslant m}B_{j}<0\right)+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{2\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{\sigma\sqrt{2\pi m}}\int_{t-m^{1/2-2\varepsilon}}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{s^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}m}}ds+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{2\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle\leqslant c\int_{\frac{t}{2\sigma\sqrt{m}}}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{u^{2}}{2}}du+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{2\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle\leqslant ce^{-\frac{t^{2}}{8\sigma^{2}\sqrt{\varepsilon}n}}+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{2\varepsilon}},

where in the last line we used the inequality
\int_{a}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{u^{2}}{2}}du\leqslant\frac{1}{a}e^{-\frac{a^{2}}{2}}
for
a>0
,
and the fact that
\frac{\sqrt{m}}{t}=\frac{\sqrt{[\varepsilon n]}}{t}\leqslant 1
for
t\geqslant\varepsilon^{1/4}\sqrt{n}
.
Hence,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{1/2}\left(t+\min_{1\leqslant j\leqslant m}S_{j}<0\right)\leqslant ce^{-\frac{t^{2}}{16\sigma^{2}\sqrt{\varepsilon}n}}+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon}}.

This, together with (
3.35
) and (
3.5
), implies that

(3.41)

\displaystyle J_{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant c\varepsilon^{1/12}\int_{\varepsilon^{1/4}\sqrt{n}}^{\infty}g(t+\varepsilon)e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{16\sigma^{2}\sqrt{\varepsilon}n}}dt+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon}}\|g\|_{1}.

Putting (
3.38
) and (
3.41
) together
and using the fact that
e^{\frac{t^{2}}{16\sigma^{2}\sqrt{\varepsilon}n}}\leqslant c
for any
t\in[0,\varepsilon^{1/4}\sqrt{n}]
,
we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}M(t)\phi^{+}\left(\frac{t}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\right)dt

\displaystyle\leqslant c\varepsilon^{1/12}\left[\int_{0}^{\varepsilon^{1/4}\sqrt{n}}g(t+\varepsilon)dt+\int_{\varepsilon^{1/4}\sqrt{n}}^{\infty}g(t+\varepsilon)e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{16\sigma^{2}\sqrt{\varepsilon}n}}dt\right]+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon}}\|g\|_{1}

(3.42)

\displaystyle\leqslant c\varepsilon^{1/12}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}g(t+\varepsilon)e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{16\sigma^{2}\sqrt{\varepsilon}n}}dt+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon}}\|g\|_{1}.

For the second term on the right hand side of (
3.5
),
following the same proof of (
3.5
) and using the fact that
e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}\varepsilon^{1/2}n}}\leqslant 1
,
one has

(3.43)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}M(t)e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}\varepsilon^{1/2}n}}dt\leqslant c\varepsilon^{1/12}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}g(t+\varepsilon)e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{16\sigma^{2}\sqrt{\varepsilon}n}}dt+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon}}\|g\|_{1}.

Implementing (
3.5
) and (
3.43
) into (
3.5
) gives

(3.44)

\displaystyle K_{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant c\varepsilon^{1/12}\frac{V(x)}{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}g(t+\varepsilon)e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{16\sigma^{2}\sqrt{\varepsilon}n}}dt+c_{\varepsilon}\left(\alpha_{n}+n^{-\varepsilon}\right)\frac{V(x)}{n}\|g\|_{1}.

From
(
3.5
), (
3.29
) and (
3.44
),
and using the fact that
e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{16\sigma^{2}\sqrt{\varepsilon}n}}\leqslant c\phi(\frac{t}{\sigma\sqrt{n}})
, we obtain

\displaystyle I_{n,2}(x)

\displaystyle\leqslant c\varepsilon^{1/12}\frac{V(x)}{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}}[g(t)+g(t+\varepsilon)]\phi\left(\frac{t}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)dt

\displaystyle\qquad+c_{\varepsilon}\left(\alpha_{n}+n^{-\varepsilon}\right)\frac{V(x)}{n}\|g\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leqslant c\varepsilon^{1/12}\frac{V(x)}{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}}g(t)\phi\left(\frac{t}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)dt+c_{\varepsilon}\left(\alpha_{n}+n^{-\varepsilon}\right)\frac{V(x)}{n}\|g\|_{1},

where in the last line we used the Lipschitz continuity of
\phi
.
Combining this with (
3.5
) and (
3.5
), and using the fact that
f\leqslant_{\varepsilon}g
,
we conclude the proof of the lower bound (
3.1
).

3.6.
Proof of Theorems
1.1
and
1.2

In this section we show how to deduce Theorems
1.1
and
1.2
from Theorem
3.1
.

Proof of Theorem
1.1
.

We first prove that (
1.5
) holds
uniformly in
x\in[0,n^{1/2-\varepsilon}]
and
y\in[\eta\sqrt{n},\sigma\sqrt{qn\log n}]
.
Without loss of generality, we assume that the target function
f
is non-negative.
Since
f\leqslant_{\varepsilon}\overline{f}_{\delta,\varepsilon}
with
\overline{f}_{\delta,\varepsilon}
defined by (
2.4
),
applying the upper bound (
3.1
) of Theorem
3.1
with
g=\overline{f}_{\delta,\varepsilon}
and
\alpha_{n}=n^{-\varepsilon}
,
we derive that for any
x\in[0,n^{1/2-\varepsilon}]
and
y\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(f(x+S_{n}-y);\tau_{x}>n\right)\leqslant(1+c\varepsilon)\frac{2V(x)}{\sqrt{2\pi}\sigma^{2}n}J_{n},

where

\displaystyle J_{n}=\int_{-\varepsilon}^{\infty}\overline{f}_{\delta,\varepsilon}(t)\left[\phi^{+}\left(\frac{t+y}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)+\phi\left(\frac{t+y}{\varepsilon^{1/4}\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon}}\right]dt.

Since the functions
\phi^{+}
and
\phi
are Lipschitz continuous on
\mathbb{R}
, there exists a constant
c>0
such that for any

t\geqslant-\varepsilon
and
y\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\left|\phi^{+}\left(\frac{t+y}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)-\phi^{+}\left(\frac{y}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leqslant c\frac{|t|}{\sqrt{n}},

\displaystyle\left|\phi\left(\frac{t+y}{\varepsilon^{1/4}\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)-\phi\left(\frac{y}{\varepsilon^{1/4}\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leqslant c\frac{|t|}{\varepsilon^{1/4}\sqrt{n}},

which implies that

\displaystyle J_{n}

\displaystyle\leqslant\left[\phi^{+}\left(\frac{y}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)+\phi\left(\frac{y}{\varepsilon^{1/4}\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon}}\right]\int_{-\varepsilon}^{\infty}\overline{f}_{\delta,\varepsilon}(t)dt

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{c}{\varepsilon^{1/4}\sqrt{n}}\int_{-\varepsilon}^{\infty}\overline{f}_{\delta,\varepsilon}(t)|t|dt.

Note that for any fixed
\eta>0
, there exists a constant
c_{\eta}>0
such that
uniformly in
y\in[\eta\sqrt{n},\sigma\sqrt{qn\log n}]
,

(3.45)

\displaystyle n^{-q/2}\leqslant c_{\eta}\phi^{+}\left(\frac{y}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right),\quad\phi\left(\frac{y}{\varepsilon^{1/4}\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)\leqslant c_{\eta}\exp\Big\{-\frac{\eta^{2}}{4\sqrt{\varepsilon}\sigma^{2}}\Big\}\phi^{+}\left(\frac{y}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right).

It follows that uniformly in
y\in[\eta\sqrt{n},\sigma\sqrt{qn\log n}]
,

\displaystyle J_{n}

\displaystyle\leqslant\phi^{+}\left(\frac{y}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)\left(1+c_{\eta}\exp\Big\{-\frac{\eta^{2}}{4\sqrt{\varepsilon}\sigma^{2}}\Big\}+\frac{c_{\varepsilon,\eta}}{n^{\varepsilon-q/2}}\right)\int_{-\varepsilon}^{\infty}\overline{f}_{\delta,\varepsilon}(t)dt

\displaystyle\quad+\phi^{+}\left(\frac{y}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)\frac{c_{\eta}}{\varepsilon^{1/4}n^{1/2-q/2}}\int_{-\varepsilon}^{\infty}\overline{f}_{\delta,\varepsilon}(t)|t|dt.

Therefore, we get that uniformly in
x\in[0,n^{1/2-\varepsilon}]
and
y\in[\eta\sqrt{n},\sigma\sqrt{qn\log n}]
,

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mathbb{E}\left(f(x+S_{n}-y);\tau_{x}>n\right)}{\frac{2V(x)}{\sqrt{2\pi}\sigma^{2}n}\phi^{+}\left(\frac{y}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)}\leqslant\left[1+c_{\eta}\exp\Big\{-\frac{\eta^{2}}{4\sqrt{\varepsilon}\sigma^{2}}\Big\}\right]\int_{-\varepsilon}^{\infty}\overline{f}_{\delta,\varepsilon}(t)dt.

This proves the upper bound by
taking first
\varepsilon\to 0
and then
\delta\to 0
, and using Lemma
2.1
.
The proof of the lower bound can be carried out in the same way
using (
3.1
).

In a similar way, one can also use Theorem
3.1
to prove that
(
1.5
) holds uniformly in
x\in[0,\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}]
and

y\in[\eta\sqrt{n},\eta^{-1}\sqrt{n}]
.
The proof of Theorem
1.1
is complete.
∎

We will show in Lemma
7.4
that the harmonic function
V
satisfies
x\leqslant V(x)\leqslant c(1+x)
for any
x\geqslant 0
.
To prove Theorem
1.2
we need the following refinement of this bound.

Lemma 3.7
.

Assume
2
for some
\delta>0
.
Then, for any
\varepsilon\in(0,\frac{\delta}{2(2+\delta)}]
,
there exists a constant
c_{\varepsilon}>0
such that
for any
x\geqslant 0
and
k_{0}\geqslant 1
,

\displaystyle x\leqslant V(x)\leqslant\left(1+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{k_{0}^{\varepsilon}}\right)x+c_{\varepsilon}k_{0}^{1/2-\varepsilon}.

Proof of Theorem
1.2
.

We first prove that (
1.6
) holds
uniformly in
x\in[0,n^{1/2-\varepsilon}]
,
y\in[\eta\sqrt{n},\sigma\sqrt{qn\log n}]

and
\Delta\in[\Delta_{0},n^{1/2-\varepsilon}]
.
Using the upper bound (
3.1
)
with
f=\mathds{1}_{[y,y+\Delta]}
,
g=\mathds{1}_{[y-\varepsilon,y+\Delta+\varepsilon]}

and
\alpha_{n}=n^{-\varepsilon}
, we have, uniformly in
x\in[0,n^{1/2-\varepsilon}]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(x+S_{n}\in[0,\Delta]+y,\,\tau_{x}>n\right)\leqslant(1+c\varepsilon)\frac{2V\left(x\right)}{\sqrt{2\pi}\sigma^{2}n}J_{n},

where

\displaystyle J_{n}=\int_{-\varepsilon}^{\Delta+\varepsilon}\left[\phi^{+}\left(\frac{t+y}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)+\phi\left(\frac{t+y}{\varepsilon^{1/4}\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon}}\right]dt.

Since the functions
\phi^{+}
and
\phi
are Lipschitz continuous on
\mathbb{R}
, there exists a constant
c>0

such that for any
\Delta\in[\Delta_{0},n^{1/2-\varepsilon}]
,

t\in[-\varepsilon,\Delta+\varepsilon]
and
y\in[\eta\sqrt{n},\sigma\sqrt{qn\log n}]
,

\displaystyle\left|\phi^{+}\left(\frac{t+y}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)-\phi^{+}\left(\frac{y}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leqslant c\frac{|t|}{\sqrt{n}}\leqslant\frac{c}{n^{\varepsilon}},

\displaystyle\left|\phi\left(\frac{t+y}{\varepsilon^{1/4}\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)-\phi\left(\frac{y}{\varepsilon^{1/4}\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leqslant c\frac{|t|}{\varepsilon^{1/4}\sqrt{n}}\leqslant\frac{c}{\varepsilon^{1/4}n^{\varepsilon}}.

This implies that there exists a constant
c_{\eta}>0
such that
uniformly in
y\in[\eta\sqrt{n},\sigma\sqrt{qn\log n}]
and
\Delta\in[\Delta_{0},n^{1/2-\varepsilon}]
,

\displaystyle J_{n}

\displaystyle\leqslant(\Delta+2\varepsilon)\left[\phi^{+}\left(\frac{y}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)+\phi\left(\frac{y}{\varepsilon^{1/4}\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)+\frac{c}{\varepsilon^{1/4}n^{\varepsilon}}\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant\Delta(1+c\varepsilon)\phi^{+}\left(\frac{y}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)\left(1+c_{\eta}\exp\left\{-\frac{\eta^{2}}{4\sqrt{\varepsilon}\sigma^{2}}\right\}+\frac{c_{\eta}}{\varepsilon^{1/4}n^{\varepsilon-q/2}}\right),

where in the last inequality we used (
3.45
) and the fact that
\Delta_{0}>0
is a fixed constant.
Therefore, we get that uniformly in
x\in[0,n^{1/2-\varepsilon}]
,
y\in[\eta\sqrt{n},\sigma\sqrt{qn\log n}]

and
\Delta\in[\Delta_{0},n^{1/2-\varepsilon}]
,

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mathbb{P}\left(x+S_{n}\in[0,\Delta]+y,\,\tau_{x}>n\right)}{\Delta\frac{2V\left(x\right)}{\sqrt{2\pi}\sigma^{2}n}\phi^{+}\left(\frac{y}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)}\leqslant 1+c_{\eta}\exp\left\{-\frac{\eta^{2}}{4\sqrt{\varepsilon}\sigma^{2}}\right\}.

Since
\varepsilon>0
can be arbitrary small, this proves the upper bound.
The proof of the lower bound can be carried out in the same way
using (
3.1
).

Similarly, one can use Theorem
3.1
to prove that
the asymptotic (
1.6
) holds uniformly in
x\in[0,\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}]
,

y\in[\eta\sqrt{n},\eta^{-1}\sqrt{n}]
and
\Delta\in[\Delta_{0},\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}]
.
Hence the proof Theorem
1.2
is complete.
∎

4.
Conditioned local limit theorem near the boundary

The aim of this section is to establish Theorems
1.3
and
1.4
.

4.1.
A non-asymptotic conditioned local limit theorem

The following bounds will be used the proofs of
Theorems
1.3
and
1.4
.
It is an easy consequence of the duality formula (Lemma
3.2
)
and bounds of the exit time for the dual random walk.

Lemma 4.1
.

Assume
2
.
Let
g:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
be a measurable function
satisfying
\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}g(y)(1+y)dy<\infty
.
Then there exists a constant
c
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{n\geqslant 1}\sqrt{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\mathbb{E}(g(x+S_{n});\tau_{x}>n)dx\leqslant c\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}g(y)(1+y)dy.

Moreover,

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\sqrt{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\mathbb{E}(g(x+S_{n});\tau_{x}>n)dx=\frac{2}{\sigma\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}g(y)V^{*}(y)dy.

Proof.

Using Lemma
3.2
with
h=1
, we get that for any
n\geqslant 1
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\mathbb{E}(g(x+S_{n});\tau_{x}>n)dx

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}g(y)\mathbb{P}\big(\tau_{y}^{\ast}>n\big)dy.

The conclusion follows from (
2.22
),
(
2.23
) of Theorem
2.7

and the Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem.
∎

Theorems
1.3
and
1.4

will be deduced from the following more general statement
in which we establish upper and lower bounds for the joint law of the random walk
x+S_{n}
and of the event
\{\tau_{x}>n\}
.

Theorem 4.2
.

Assume
1
and
2
.
Then, there exist constants
c>0
and
\varepsilon_{0}>0
such that for any
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})

and any sequence of positive numbers
(\alpha_{n})_{n\geqslant 1}
satisfying
\lim_{n\to\infty}\alpha_{n}=0
,
one can find a constant
c_{\varepsilon}>0
such that
uniformly in
x\in[0,\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}]
and
n\geqslant 1
, the following holds:

1. For any measurable functions
f,g:\mathbb{R}\mapsto\mathbb{R}_{+}
satisfying
f\leqslant_{\varepsilon}g
and

\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}g(t-\varepsilon)(1+t)dt<\infty
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(f\left(x+S_{n}\right);\tau_{x}>n\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{2V(x)}{\sqrt{2\pi}\sigma^{3}n^{3/2}}\int_{0}^{\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}}g(t-\varepsilon)V^{*}(t)dt

\displaystyle\quad+\left(c\sqrt{\varepsilon}+c_{\varepsilon}\alpha_{n}+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon}}\right)\frac{V(x)}{n^{3/2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}g(t-\varepsilon)(1+t)dt

(4.1)

\displaystyle\quad+c\frac{V(x)}{n}\int_{\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}}^{\infty}g(t-\varepsilon)dt.

2. For any measurable functions
f,g,h:\mathbb{R}\mapsto\mathbb{R}_{+}

satisfying
h\leqslant_{\varepsilon}f\leqslant_{\varepsilon}g
and
\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}g(t-\varepsilon)(1+t)dt<\infty
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(f\left(x+S_{n}\right);\tau_{x}>n\right)

\displaystyle\geqslant\frac{2V(x)}{\sqrt{2\pi}\sigma^{3}n^{3/2}}\int_{0}^{\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}}h(t+\varepsilon)V^{*}(t)dt

\displaystyle\quad-\left(c\varepsilon^{1/12}+c_{\varepsilon}\alpha_{n}+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon}}\right)\frac{V(x)}{n^{3/2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}g(t-\varepsilon)(1+t)dt

(4.2)

\displaystyle\quad-c\varepsilon^{1/12}\frac{V(x)}{n}\int_{\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}}^{\infty}h(t-\varepsilon)dt.

Proof.

We first establish the upper bound (
4.2
).
Set
m=\left[n/2\right]
and
k=n-m.

By the Markov property, we have for any
x\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
,

(4.3)

\displaystyle I_{n}(x):=\mathbb{E}\left(f(x+S_{n});\tau_{x}>n\right)=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}I_{m}(t)\mathbb{P}\left(x+S_{k}\in dt,\tau_{x}>k\right).

When
x<0
, let
I_{n}(x)=0
for any
n\geqslant 1
.
Now we are going to find an
\varepsilon
-domination for the function
t\mapsto I_{m}(t)

given on the right hand side of (
4.3
).
Since
f\leqslant_{\varepsilon}g
, it holds that for any
t\in\mathbb{R}
and
|v|\leqslant\varepsilon
,

(4.4)

\displaystyle I_{m}(t)=\mathbb{E}\left(f\left(t+S_{m}\right);\tau_{t}>m\right)\leqslant H_{m}(t+v),

where

(4.5)

\displaystyle H_{m}(t):=\mathbb{E}\left(g\left(t+S_{m}\right);\tau_{t+\varepsilon}>m\right)\mathds{1}_{\{t\geqslant-\varepsilon\}},\quad t\in\mathbb{R}.

It is easy to see that
I_{m}\leqslant_{\varepsilon}H_{m}
and that both
I_{m}
and
H_{m}
are integrable on
\mathbb{R}
.
Thus we can apply the upper bound (
3.1
) of Theorem
3.1
to obtain

\displaystyle I_{n}(x)\leqslant(1+c\varepsilon)\frac{2V(x)}{\sqrt{2\pi}\sigma^{2}k}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}H_{m}\left(t\right)\phi^{+}\left(\frac{t}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\right)dt

\displaystyle\quad+c\varepsilon^{1/4}\frac{V(x)}{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}}H_{m}(t)\phi\left(\frac{t}{\varepsilon^{1/4}\sigma\sqrt{k}}\right)dt+c_{\varepsilon}\left(\alpha_{n}+n^{-\varepsilon}\right)\frac{V(x)}{n}\left\|H_{m}\right\|_{1}

(4.6)

\displaystyle=:(1+c\varepsilon)J_{1}+J_{2}+J_{3}.

Bound of
J_{1}
.

We use a change of variable and the duality formula (Lemma
3.2
) to get

\displaystyle J_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{2V(x)}{\sqrt{2\pi}\sigma^{2}k}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\mathbb{E}(g(t+S_{m});\tau_{t+\varepsilon}>m)\phi^{+}\left(\frac{t}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\right)dt

\displaystyle=\frac{2V(x)}{\sqrt{2\pi}\sigma^{2}k}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\mathbb{E}(g(t+S_{m}-\varepsilon);\tau_{t}>m)\phi^{+}\left(\frac{t-\varepsilon}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\right)dt

(4.7)

\displaystyle=\frac{2V(x)}{\sqrt{2\pi}\sigma^{2}k}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}g(t-\varepsilon)\mathbb{E}\left[\phi^{+}\left(\frac{t+S_{m}^{*}-\varepsilon}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\right);\tau_{t}^{*}>m\right]dt.

Then we split this integral into two parts:
J_{1}=J_{11}+J_{12}
, where

(4.8)

\displaystyle J_{11}

\displaystyle=\frac{2V(x)}{\sqrt{2\pi}\sigma^{2}k}\int_{\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}}^{\infty}g(t-\varepsilon)\mathbb{E}\left[\phi^{+}\left(\frac{t+S_{m}^{*}-\varepsilon}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\right);\tau_{t}^{*}>m\right]dt,

(4.9)

\displaystyle J_{12}

\displaystyle=\frac{2V(x)}{\sqrt{2\pi}\sigma^{2}k}\int_{0}^{\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}}g(t-\varepsilon)\mathbb{E}\left[\phi^{+}\left(\frac{t+S_{m}^{*}-\varepsilon}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\right);\tau_{t}^{*}>m\right]dt.

Bound of
J_{11}
.

Since the function
\phi^{+}
is bounded on
\mathbb{R}
, we easily get

(4.10)

\displaystyle J_{11}\leqslant c\frac{V(x)}{n}\int_{\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}}^{\infty}g(t-\varepsilon)dt.

Bound of
J_{12}
.

Since the function
\phi^{+}
is differentiable on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
, using integration by parts leads to

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\phi^{+}\left(\frac{t+S_{m}^{*}-\varepsilon}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\right);\tau_{t}^{*}>m\right]

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(\phi^{+})^{\prime}(u)\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{t+S_{m}^{*}-\varepsilon}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}>u,\,\tau_{t}^{*}>m\right)du

(4.11)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(\phi^{+})^{\prime}(u)\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{t+S_{m}^{*}}{\sigma\sqrt{m}}>u_{k,m,\varepsilon},\,\tau_{t}^{*}>m\right)du,

where
u_{k,m,\varepsilon}=\frac{u\sigma\sqrt{k}+\varepsilon}{\sigma\sqrt{m}}
.
Applying the conditioned integral limit theorem (
2.26
) of Corollary
2.9

(with
S_{m}
,
\tau_{t}
and
V(t)
replaced by
S_{m}^{*}
,
\tau_{t}^{*}
and
V^{*}(t)
, respectively),
we get that uniformly in
t\in[0,\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}]
and
u\geqslant 0
,

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{t+S_{m}^{*}}{\sigma\sqrt{m}}>u_{k,m,\varepsilon},\,\tau_{t}^{*}>m\right)-\frac{2V^{*}(t)}{\sigma\sqrt{2\pi m}}\left(1-\Phi^{+}\left(u_{k,m,\varepsilon}\right)\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leqslant c_{\varepsilon}\left(\alpha_{n}+n^{-\varepsilon}\right)\frac{V^{*}(t)}{n^{1/2}}.

Substituting this into (
4.1
) and using the fact that
\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|(\phi^{+})^{\prime}(u)|du<\infty
,
we obtain that uniformly in
t\in[0,\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}]
,

\displaystyle\Bigg|

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\phi^{+}\left(\frac{t+S_{m}^{*}-\varepsilon}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\right);\tau_{t}^{*}>m)\right]

(4.12)

\displaystyle-\frac{2V^{*}(t)}{\sigma\sqrt{2\pi m}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(\phi^{+})^{\prime}(u)\left[1-\Phi^{+}\left(u_{k,m,\varepsilon}\right)\right]du\Bigg|\leqslant c_{\varepsilon}\left(\alpha_{n}+n^{-\varepsilon}\right)\frac{V^{*}(t)}{n^{1/2}}.

Since
m=\left[n/2\right]
,
k=n-m
and
u_{k,m,\varepsilon}=\frac{u\sigma\sqrt{k}+\varepsilon}{\sigma\sqrt{m}}
,
using integration by parts and the fact that
|(\phi^{+})^{\prime}|\leqslant c
for some constant
c>0
, we get

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(\phi^{+})^{\prime}(u)\left(1-\Phi^{+}\left(u_{k,m,\varepsilon}\right)\right)du

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{k}{m}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\phi^{+}(u)\phi^{+}\left(u_{k,m,\varepsilon}\right)du

(4.13)

\displaystyle\leqslant\sqrt{\frac{k}{m}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\phi^{+}(u)\phi^{+}\left(\frac{u\sqrt{k}}{\sqrt{m}}\right)du+\frac{c\varepsilon}{\sqrt{n}}.

By a change of variable and the fact that
k+m=n
, we see that

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{k}{m}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\phi^{+}(u)\phi^{+}\left(\frac{u\sqrt{k}}{\sqrt{m}}\right)du=\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\phi^{+}\left(\frac{y}{\sqrt{k}}\right)\phi^{+}\left(\frac{y}{\sqrt{m}}\right)dy

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\frac{y^{2}}{\sqrt{km}}e^{-\frac{ny^{2}}{2km}}dy=\frac{k\sqrt{m}}{n^{3/2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}y^{2}e^{-y^{2}/2}dy=\frac{k\sqrt{2\pi m}}{2n^{3/2}}.

Therefore, combining this with (
4.9
), (
4.1
) and (
4.1
),
we obtain that uniformly in
t\in[0,\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}]
,

\displaystyle J_{12}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{2V(x)}{\sqrt{2\pi}\sigma^{2}k}\int_{0}^{\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}}g(t-\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad\times\left[\frac{2V^{*}(t)}{\sigma\sqrt{2\pi m}}\frac{k\sqrt{2\pi m}}{2n^{3/2}}+\left(c\frac{\varepsilon}{\sqrt{n}}+c_{\varepsilon}\alpha_{n}+c_{\varepsilon}n^{-\varepsilon}\right)\frac{V^{*}(t)}{\sqrt{n}}\right]dt

(4.14)

\displaystyle\leqslant\left(1+c_{\varepsilon}\alpha_{n}+c_{\varepsilon}n^{-\varepsilon}\right)\frac{2V(x)}{\sqrt{2\pi}\sigma^{3}n^{3/2}}\int_{0}^{\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}}g(t-\varepsilon)V^{*}(t)dt.

Bound of
J_{2}
.
The estimate of
J_{2}
(cf. (
4.1
)) follows the same lines as that of
J_{1}
.
Proceeding in the same way as the proof of (
4.1
), (
4.8
) and (
4.9
), one has
J_{2}=J_{21}+J_{22}
, where

\displaystyle J_{21}

\displaystyle=c\varepsilon^{1/4}\frac{V(x)}{n}\int_{\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}}^{\infty}g(t-\varepsilon)\mathbb{E}\left[\phi\left(\frac{t+S_{m}^{*}-\varepsilon}{\varepsilon^{1/4}\sigma\sqrt{k}}\right);\tau_{t}^{*}>m\right]dt,

\displaystyle J_{22}

\displaystyle=c\varepsilon^{1/4}\frac{V(x)}{n}\int_{0}^{\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}}g(t-\varepsilon)\mathbb{E}\left[\phi\left(\frac{t+S_{m}^{*}-\varepsilon}{\varepsilon^{1/4}\sigma\sqrt{k}}\right);\tau_{t}^{*}>m\right]dt.

Bound of
J_{21}
.
As in (
4.10
), since the function
\phi
is bounded on
\mathbb{R}
, we have

(4.15)

\displaystyle J_{21}\leqslant c\varepsilon^{1/4}\frac{V(x)}{n}\int_{\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}}^{\infty}g(t-\varepsilon)dt.

Bound of
J_{22}
.

Similarly to (
4.1
), it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\phi\left(\frac{t+S_{m}^{*}-\varepsilon}{\varepsilon^{1/4}\sigma\sqrt{k}}\right);\tau_{t}^{*}>m\right]=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\phi^{\prime}(u)\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{t+S_{m}^{*}}{\sigma\sqrt{m}}>\tilde{u}_{k,m,\varepsilon},\,\tau_{t}^{*}>m\right)du,

where
\tilde{u}_{k,m,\varepsilon}=\frac{\varepsilon^{1/4}\sigma\sqrt{k}u+\varepsilon}{\sigma\sqrt{m}}
.
Following the proof of (
4.1
), we derive that uniformly in
t\in[0,\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\phi\left(\frac{t+S_{m}^{*}-\varepsilon}{\varepsilon^{1/4}\sigma\sqrt{k}}\right);\tau_{t}^{*}>m\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{2V^{*}(t)}{\sigma\sqrt{2\pi m}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\phi^{\prime}(u)\left[1-\Phi^{+}\left(\tilde{u}_{k,m,\varepsilon}\right)\right]du+c_{\varepsilon}\left(\alpha_{n}+n^{-\varepsilon}\right)\frac{V^{*}(t)}{n^{1/2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{2V^{*}(t)}{\sigma\sqrt{2\pi m}}\varepsilon^{1/4}\sqrt{\frac{k}{m}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\phi(u)\phi^{+}\left(\tilde{u}_{k,m,\varepsilon}\right)du+c_{\varepsilon}\left(\alpha_{n}+n^{-\varepsilon}\right)\frac{V^{*}(t)}{n^{1/2}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\left(c\varepsilon^{1/4}+c_{\varepsilon}\alpha_{n}+c_{\varepsilon}n^{-\varepsilon}\right)\frac{V^{*}(t)}{\sqrt{n}},

which implies that

(4.16)

\displaystyle J_{22}\leqslant\left(c\sqrt{\varepsilon}+c_{\varepsilon}\alpha_{n}+c_{\varepsilon}n^{-\varepsilon}\right)\frac{V(x)}{n^{3/2}}\int_{0}^{\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}}g(t-\varepsilon)V^{*}(t)dt.

Bound of
J_{3}
.

By (
4.5
), a change of variable and Lemma
4.1
, we have

(4.17)

\displaystyle\left\|H_{m}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\mathbb{E}\left(g\left(t-\varepsilon+S_{m}\right);\tau_{t}>m\right)dt\leqslant\frac{c}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}g(t-\varepsilon)(1+t)dt.

Since
m=[n/2]
, it follows from (
4.1
) and (
4.17
) that

(4.18)

\displaystyle J_{3}

\displaystyle\leqslant c_{\varepsilon}\left(\alpha_{n}+n^{-\varepsilon}\right)\frac{V(x)}{n^{3/2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}g(t-\varepsilon)(1+t)dt.

Combining (
4.10
), (
4.1
), (
4.15
),
(
4.16
) and (
4.18
)
concludes the proof of the upper bound (
4.2
) of the theorem.

We next sketch the proof of the lower bound (
4.2
).
Similarly to (
4.1
), we use the lower bound (
3.1
) of Theorem
3.1
to get
that uniformly in
x\in[0,\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}]
,

\displaystyle I_{n}(x)

\displaystyle\geqslant\frac{2V(x)}{\sqrt{2\pi}\sigma^{2}k}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}L_{m}\left(t\right)\phi^{+}\left(\frac{t}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\right)dt

\displaystyle\quad-c\varepsilon^{1/12}\frac{V(x)}{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}}H_{m}(t)\left[\phi\left(\frac{t}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\right)+\phi^{+}\left(\frac{t}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\right)\right]dt

\displaystyle\quad-c_{\varepsilon}\left(\alpha_{n}+n^{-\varepsilon}\right)\frac{V(x)}{n}\left\|H_{m}\right\|_{1}

(4.19)

\displaystyle=:K_{1}+K_{2}+K_{3},

where
L_{m}\leqslant_{\varepsilon}I_{m}\leqslant_{\varepsilon}H_{m}
with
H_{m}
defined by (
4.5
) and

(4.20)

\displaystyle L_{m}(t):=\mathbb{E}\left(h\left(t+S_{m}\right);\tau_{t-\varepsilon}>m\right)\mathds{1}_{\{t\geqslant\varepsilon\}},\quad t\in\mathbb{R}.

Bound of
K_{1}
.

In the same way as in the proof of (
4.1
),
using the duality formula (Lemma
3.2
), one has

\displaystyle K_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{2V(x)}{\sqrt{2\pi}\sigma^{2}k}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}h(t+\varepsilon)\mathbb{E}\left[\phi^{+}\left(\frac{t+S_{m}^{*}+\varepsilon}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\right);\tau_{t}^{*}>m\right]dt

\displaystyle\geqslant\frac{2V(x)}{\sqrt{2\pi}\sigma^{2}k}\int_{0}^{\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}}h(t+\varepsilon)\mathbb{E}\left[\phi^{+}\left(\frac{t+S_{m}^{*}+\varepsilon}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\right);\tau_{t}^{*}>m\right]dt.

Following the proof of (
4.1
), we get that uniformly in
x\in[0,\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}]
,

\displaystyle K_{1}\geqslant\left(1-c_{\varepsilon}\alpha_{n}-c_{\varepsilon}n^{-\varepsilon}\right)\frac{2V(x)}{\sqrt{2\pi}\sigma^{3}n^{3/2}}\int_{0}^{\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}}h(t+\varepsilon)V^{*}(t)dt.

Bound of
K_{2}
.
Following the proof of (
4.15
) and (
4.16
),
one can check that

\displaystyle K_{2}

\displaystyle\geqslant-c\varepsilon^{1/12}\frac{V(x)}{n}\int_{\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}}^{\infty}h(t-\varepsilon)dt

\displaystyle\quad-\left(c\varepsilon^{1/12}+c_{\varepsilon}\alpha_{n}+c_{\varepsilon}n^{-\varepsilon}\right)\frac{V(x)}{n^{3/2}}\int_{0}^{\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}}g(t-\varepsilon)V^{*}(t)dt.

Bound of
K_{3}
.
From (
4.18
) we see that

\displaystyle K_{3}\geqslant-c_{\varepsilon}\left(\alpha_{n}+n^{-\varepsilon}\right)\frac{V(x)}{n^{3/2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}g(t-\varepsilon)(1+t)dt.

Collecting the above bounds for
K_{1}
,
K_{2}
and
K_{3}
,
and using the fact that
h(t+\varepsilon)\leqslant f(t)\leqslant g(t-\varepsilon)
for any
t\in\mathbb{R}
, we get the lower bound (
4.2
).
∎

4.2.
Proof of Theorems
1.3
and
1.4

Proof of Theorem
1.3
.

We first give a proof of (
1.9
).
By the assumption
\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}f(t)(1+t)^{\gamma}dt<\infty
for some constant
\gamma>1
,
the asymptotic (
1.9
) is a consequence of Theorem
4.2
,
Lemma
2.1
and the Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem.

We next give a proof of (
1.10
).
Applying the upper bound (
4.2
) of Theorem
4.2

with
g=\overline{f}_{\delta,\varepsilon}
(cf. (
2.4
))
and
\alpha_{n}
replaced by
2\alpha_{n}
,
we get that uniformly in
x\in[0,\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}]
and
y\in[\alpha_{n}^{-1},\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(f(x+S_{n}-y);\tau_{x}>n\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{2V\left(x\right)}{\sqrt{2\pi}\sigma^{3}n^{3/2}}\int_{0}^{2\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}}\overline{f}_{\delta,\varepsilon}(t-y-\varepsilon)V^{*}(t)dt

\displaystyle\quad+\left(c\sqrt{\varepsilon}+c_{\varepsilon}\alpha_{n}+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon}}\right)\frac{V(x)}{n^{3/2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\overline{f}_{\delta,\varepsilon}(t-y-\varepsilon)(1+t)dt

(4.21)

\displaystyle\quad+c\frac{V(x)}{n}\int_{2\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}}^{\infty}\overline{f}_{\delta,\varepsilon}(t-y-\varepsilon)dt.

We claim that as
n\to\infty
, it holds uniformly in
y\in[\alpha_{n}^{-1},\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}]
that

(4.22)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}}\overline{f}_{\delta,\varepsilon}(t-y-\varepsilon)V^{*}(t)dt

\displaystyle\sim y\int_{-\varepsilon}^{\infty}\overline{f}_{\delta,\varepsilon}(u)du,

(4.23)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\overline{f}_{\delta,\varepsilon}(t-y-\varepsilon)(1+t)dt

\displaystyle\sim y\int_{-\varepsilon}^{\infty}\overline{f}_{\delta,\varepsilon}(u)du,

(4.24)

\displaystyle\int_{2\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}}^{\infty}\overline{f}_{\delta,\varepsilon}(t-y-\varepsilon)dt

\displaystyle\sim\frac{y}{\sqrt{n}}o(1).

Indeed, by a change of variable and the fact that the function
\overline{f}_{\delta,\varepsilon}
is supported on
[-\varepsilon,\infty)
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}}\overline{f}_{\delta,\varepsilon}(t-y-\varepsilon)V^{*}(t)dt

\displaystyle=\int_{-\varepsilon}^{2\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}-y-\varepsilon}\overline{f}_{\delta,\varepsilon}(u)V^{*}(u+y+\varepsilon)du

\displaystyle=\int_{-\varepsilon}^{\infty}\overline{f}_{\delta,\varepsilon}(u)V^{*}(u+y+\varepsilon)du

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{2\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}-y-\varepsilon}^{\infty}\overline{f}_{\delta,\varepsilon}(u)V^{*}(u+y+\varepsilon)du.

Since
V^{*}(t)/t\to\infty
as
t\to\infty

and
\int_{-\varepsilon}^{\infty}\overline{f}_{\delta,\varepsilon}(u)(1+u)du<\infty
,
by the Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem, as
n\to\infty
, the first integral is equivalent to

y\int_{-\varepsilon}^{\infty}\overline{f}_{\delta,\varepsilon}(u)du
.
The second integral is negligible compared with the first one, using the inequality
t\leqslant V^{*}(t)\leqslant c(1+t)
.
Hence (
4.22
) holds and the relation (
4.23
) can be proved in the same way.
For (
4.24
), by a change of variable and the fact that
\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\overline{f}_{\delta,\varepsilon}(u)(1+u)du<\infty

and
y\in[\alpha_{n}^{-1},\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}]
with
\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}\to\infty
as
n\to\infty
,

\displaystyle\int_{2\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}}^{\infty}\overline{f}_{\delta,\varepsilon}(t-y-\varepsilon)dt

\displaystyle\leqslant\int_{\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}-\varepsilon}^{\infty}\overline{f}_{\delta,\varepsilon}(u)du

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}}\int_{\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}-\varepsilon}^{\infty}\overline{f}_{\delta,\varepsilon}(u)(1+u)du

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{y}{\sqrt{n}}o(1),

which shows (
4.24
).

Therefore, from (
4.2
), (
4.22
), (
4.23
) and (
4.24
), we get
that uniformly in
x\in[0,\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}]
and
y\in[\alpha_{n}^{-1},\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}]
,

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mathbb{E}\left(f(x+S_{n}-y);\tau_{x}>n\right)}{\frac{2yV\left(x\right)}{\sqrt{2\pi}\sigma^{3}n^{3/2}}}\leqslant\left(1+c\sqrt{\varepsilon}\right)\int_{-\varepsilon}^{\infty}\overline{f}_{\delta,\varepsilon}(u)du,

which implies the desired upper bound by taking first
\varepsilon\to 0
and then
\delta\to 0
, and using Lemma
2.1
.
The lower bound can be proved in the same way by using
(
4.2
) of Theorem
4.2
.
The proof of (
1.10
) is complete.
∎

Proof of Theorem
1.4
.

Without loss of generality, we assume that
\alpha_{n}\geqslant n^{-\varepsilon}
, so that
\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}\geqslant\Delta
,
where
\Delta\in[\Delta_{0},o(y)]
.
Applying (
4.2
) with
f=\mathds{1}_{[y,y+\Delta]}
,
g=\mathds{1}_{[y-\varepsilon,y+\Delta+\varepsilon]}

and with
\alpha_{n}
replaced by
3\alpha_{n}
,
and noticing that the last integral in (
4.2
) vanishes,
we deduce that uniformly in
x\in[0,\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}]
and
y\in[\alpha_{n}^{-1},\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(x+S_{n}\in[0,\Delta]+y,\,\tau_{x}>n\right)\leqslant\frac{2V\left(x\right)}{\sqrt{2\pi}\sigma^{3}n^{3/2}}\int_{0}^{\Delta+2\varepsilon}V^{*}(u+y)du

(4.25)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\left(c\sqrt{\varepsilon}+c_{\varepsilon}\alpha_{n}+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon}}\right)\frac{V(x)}{n^{3/2}}\int_{0}^{\Delta+2\varepsilon}(u+y+1)du.

Applying Lemma
3.7
with
k_{0}=(\frac{1}{\alpha_{n}})^{\frac{1}{1-2\varepsilon}}
,
we get that uniformly in
y\in[\alpha_{n}^{-1},\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}]
and
\Delta\in[\Delta_{0},o(y)]
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\Delta+2\varepsilon}V^{*}(u+y)du

\displaystyle\leqslant\left(1+c_{\varepsilon}\alpha_{n}^{\frac{\varepsilon}{1-2\varepsilon}}\right)\int_{0}^{\Delta+2\varepsilon}(u+y)du+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\alpha_{n}}}(\Delta+2\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\leqslant\left(1+c_{\varepsilon}\alpha_{n}^{\frac{\varepsilon}{1-2\varepsilon}}\right)y(\Delta+2\varepsilon)+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\alpha_{n}}}(\Delta+2\varepsilon)

(4.26)

\displaystyle\sim y(\Delta+2\varepsilon)\leqslant\left(1+c\varepsilon\right)y\Delta,

where in the last line we used the fact that
\frac{1}{\sqrt{\alpha_{n}}}=o(y)
and
\Delta_{0}>0
is a fixed constant.
Similarly, one can check that uniformly in
y\in[\alpha_{n}^{-1},\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}]
and
\Delta\in[\Delta_{0},o(y)]
,

(4.27)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\Delta+2\varepsilon}(u+y+1)du\leqslant\left(1+c\varepsilon\right)y\Delta.

Therefore, substituting (
4.2
) and (
4.27
) into (
4.2
),
we obtain that uniformly in
x\in[0,\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}]
,
y\in[\alpha_{n}^{-1},\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}]

and
\Delta\in[\Delta_{0},o(y)]
,

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mathbb{P}\left(x+S_{n}\in[0,\Delta]+y,\,\tau_{x}>n\right)}{\Delta\frac{2yV(x)}{\sqrt{2\pi}\sigma^{3}n^{3/2}}}\leqslant\left(1+c\sqrt{\varepsilon}\right),

which concludes the proof of the upper bound by letting
\varepsilon\to 0
.
The lower bound can be obtained in a similar way by using (
4.2
).
∎

5.
Conditioned concentration bounds far from the boundary

The goal of this section is to establish Theorems
1.5
and
1.6
.

5.1.
Formulation of the result

Denote

\displaystyle\psi^{+}(s,x)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\left(e^{-\frac{(s-x)^{2}}{2}}-e^{-\frac{(s+x)^{2}}{2}}\right)\mathds{1}_{\{s\geqslant 0\}},\quad s,x\in\mathbb{R}.

Note that
\psi^{+}(s,x)=\psi(s,x)
for any
s\geqslant 0
and
x\in\mathbb{R}
,
where
\psi
is defined by (
1.4
).
The following result is a conditioned local limit theorem of order
n^{-1}
with large starting point
x
.

Theorem 5.1
.

Assume
1
and
2
.
Then, for any
\eta>0
, there exist constants
c,\varepsilon_{0}>0
such that for any
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})
,
one can find a constant
c_{\varepsilon}>0
such that
uniformly in
x\in[n^{1/2-\varepsilon},\eta\sqrt{n}]
and
n\geqslant 1
, the following holds:

1. For any integrable functions
f,g:\mathbb{R}\mapsto\mathbb{R}_{+}
satisfying
f\leqslant_{\varepsilon}g
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(f(x+S_{n});\tau_{x}>n\right)\leqslant\frac{1+c\varepsilon}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}g(s)\psi\left(\frac{s}{\sigma\sqrt{n}},\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)ds

(5.1)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad+\frac{1+c\varepsilon}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}g(s)\left[1-\Phi\left(\frac{s}{\sigma\varepsilon^{1/4}\sqrt{n}}\right)\right]ds+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{1/2+\varepsilon}}\|g\|_{1}.

2. For any integrable functions
f,g,h:\mathbb{R}\mapsto\mathbb{R}_{+}
satisfying
h\leqslant_{\varepsilon}f\leqslant_{\varepsilon}g
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(f(x+S_{n});\tau_{x}>n\right)\geqslant\frac{1}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big[h(s)-c\varepsilon f(s)\big]\psi\left(\frac{s}{\sigma\sqrt{n}},\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)ds

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad-\frac{1}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}h(s)\left[1-\Phi\left(\frac{s}{\sigma\varepsilon^{1/4}\sqrt{n}}\right)\right]ds

(5.2)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-c\frac{\varepsilon^{1/12}}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big[g(t)+g(t+\varepsilon)\big]\phi\left(\frac{t}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)dt-\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{1/2+\varepsilon}}\|g\|_{1}.

5.2.
Proof of the upper bound

In this section we prove the upper bound (
5.1
) of Theorem
5.1
.
For any
v>0
, we denote

(5.3)

\displaystyle\psi_{v}(s,x)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi v}}\left(e^{-\frac{(s-x)^{2}}{2v}}-e^{-\frac{(s+x)^{2}}{2v}}\right),\quad s,x\in\mathbb{R}.

Then
\psi_{1}=\psi
with
\psi
defined by (
1.4
).
We shall make use of the following convolution result.
Recall that
\phi_{v}(z)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi v}}e^{-\frac{z^{2}}{2v}}
,
z\in\mathbb{R}
,
and
\Phi
is the standard normal distribution function on
\mathbb{R}
.

Lemma 5.2
.

For any
v\in(0,1)
and
s,x\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

(5.4)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi_{v}(s-z)\psi_{1-v}(z,x)dz=\psi(s,x).

In addition, for any
v\in(0,1/4]
and
s,x\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

(5.5)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{\infty}\phi_{v}(s-z)\psi_{1-v}(z,x)dz-\psi(s,x)\right|\leqslant 1-\Phi\left(\frac{s}{\sqrt{v}}\right).

Proof.

Since for any
z,x\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\psi_{1-v}(z,x)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi(1-v)}}\int_{-x}^{x}\frac{y-z}{1-v}e^{-\frac{(y-z)^{2}}{2(1-v)}}dy,

by Fubini’s theorem, we derive that for any
s\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi_{v}(s-z)\psi_{1-v}(z,x)dz=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi_{v}(z)\psi_{1-v}(s-z,x)dz

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi v}}e^{-\frac{z^{2}}{2v}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi(1-v)}}\left[\int_{-x}^{x}\frac{y-s+z}{1-v}e^{-\frac{(y-s+z)^{2}}{2(1-v)}}dy\right]dz

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}(1-v)}\int_{-x}^{x}\bigg[\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{z+y-s}{\sqrt{2\pi v(1-v)}}e^{-\frac{z^{2}}{2v}-\frac{(z+y-s)^{2}}{2(1-v)}}dz\bigg]dy.

Since

\displaystyle\frac{z^{2}}{2v}+\frac{(z+y-s)^{2}}{2(1-v)}=\frac{(z+(y-s)v)^{2}}{2v(1-v)}+\frac{(y-s)^{2}}{2},

we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi_{v}(s-z)\psi_{1-v}(z,x)dz

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}(1-v)}\int_{-x}^{x}\bigg[\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{z+y-s}{\sqrt{2\pi v(1-v)}}e^{-\frac{(z+(y-s)v)^{2}}{2v(1-v)}}dz\bigg]e^{-\frac{(y-s)^{2}}{2}}dy

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}(1-v)}\int_{-x}^{x}\bigg[-(y-s)v+(y-s)\bigg]e^{-\frac{(y-s)^{2}}{2}}dy

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\left(e^{-\frac{(s-x)^{2}}{2}}-e^{-\frac{(s+x)^{2}}{2}}\right)=\psi(s,x),

which ends the proof of (
5.4
).

To prove (
5.5
), using (
5.4
) and the fact that
\psi_{1-v}(z,x)=-\psi_{1-v}(-z,x)
,
we get that for any
s\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\phi_{v}(s-z)\psi_{1-v}(z,x)dz

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi_{v}(s-z)\psi_{1-v}(z,x)dz-\int_{-\infty}^{0}\phi_{v}(s-z)\psi_{1-v}(z,x)dz

\displaystyle=\psi(s,x)+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\phi_{v}(s+z)\psi_{1-v}(z,x)dz.

Since
\psi_{1-v}(z,x)<1
for any
v\in(0,1/4]
,
and
\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\phi_{v}(s+z)dz=1-\Phi(\frac{s}{\sqrt{v}})
for
s\in\mathbb{R}
,
the inequality (
5.5
) follows.
∎

Now we give a proof of the upper bound (
5.1
) of Theorem
5.1
.

Proof of (
5.1
).

As in the proof of (
3.1
),
let
\varepsilon>0
be a sufficiently small constant and
\delta=\sqrt{\varepsilon}
,
and set
m=\left[\delta n\right]
and
k=n-m
.
It suffices to prove (
5.1
) only for sufficiently large
n
,
otherwise the bound becomes trivial.
Similarly to the proof of (
3.4
), (
3.10
), (
3.4
), (
3.12
) and (
3.15
),
one can use the Markov property and the local limit theorem (Theorem
2.5
) to get

\displaystyle I_{n}(x):=\mathbb{E}\left(f(x+S_{n});\tau_{x}>n\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant(1+c\varepsilon)J_{n}(x)+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{1/2+\varepsilon}}\left\|g\right\|_{1}\mathbb{P}\left(\tau_{x}>k\right)

(5.6)

\displaystyle\leqslant(1+c\varepsilon)J_{n}(x)+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{1/2+\varepsilon}}\left\|g\right\|_{1},

where

(5.7)

\displaystyle J_{n}(x)

\displaystyle:=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\varphi^{\prime}_{n}(t)\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{x+S_{k}}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}>t,\tau_{x}>k\right)dt,

(5.8)

\displaystyle\varphi_{n}(t)

\displaystyle:=\int_{\mathbb{R}}g(\sigma\sqrt{k}s)\frac{1}{\sqrt{m/k}}\phi\left(\frac{t-s}{\sqrt{m/k}}\right)ds.

By the conditioned integral limit theorem (Theorem
2.8
),
we have that for any
x\geqslant n^{1/2-\varepsilon}
,

t\geqslant 0
and
n\geqslant 1
,

(5.9)

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{x+S_{k}}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}>t,\tau_{x}>k\right)-\int_{t}^{\infty}\psi\left(s,\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\right)ds\right|\leqslant\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon}}.

From (
5.7
) and (
5.9
), using integration by parts we derive that

(5.10)

\displaystyle J_{n}(x)-\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\varphi_{n}(t)\psi\left(t,\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\right)dt

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|\varphi^{\prime}_{n}(t)|dt\leqslant\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{1/2+\varepsilon}}\|g\|_{1},

where in the last inequality we used the bound (
3.4
).
By the definition of
\varphi_{n}
(cf. (
5.8
)), Fubini’s theorem and a change of variable, we get

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\varphi_{n}(t)\psi\left(t,\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\right)dt

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}}g(\sigma\sqrt{k}s)\frac{1}{\sqrt{m/k}}\phi\left(\frac{s-t}{\sqrt{m/k}}\right)\psi\left(t,\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\right)ds\right]dt

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}g(\sigma\sqrt{n}s)\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{m/n}}\phi\left(\frac{s-t}{\sqrt{m/n}}\right)\frac{1}{\sqrt{k/n}}\psi\left(\frac{t}{\sqrt{k/n}},\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\right)dt\right]ds

(5.11)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}g(\sigma\sqrt{n}s)\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\phi_{\delta_{n}}\left(s-t\right)\psi_{1-\delta_{n}}\left(t,\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)dt\right]ds,

where we denoted
\delta_{n}=\frac{m}{n}
.
By (
5.5
), we have that for any
s\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\phi_{\delta_{n}}\left(s-t\right)\psi_{1-\delta_{n}}\left(t,\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)dt\leqslant\psi\left(s,\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)+1-\Phi\left(s\sqrt{\frac{n}{m}}\right).

Thus, substituting this into (
5.2
),
we get that for any
x\geqslant n^{1/2-\varepsilon}
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\varphi_{n}(t)\psi\left(t,\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\right)dt

\displaystyle\leqslant\int_{\mathbb{R}}g(\sigma\sqrt{n}s)\left[\psi\left(s,\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)+1-\Phi\left(s\sqrt{\frac{n}{m}}\right)\right]ds

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}g(s)\psi\left(\frac{s}{\sigma\sqrt{n}},\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)ds

(5.12)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}g(s)\left[1-\Phi\left(\frac{s}{\sigma\sqrt{m}}\right)\right]ds.

Putting together (
5.2
), (
5.10
) and (
5.2
),
and recalling that
m=[\varepsilon^{1/2}n]
,
we conclude the proof of the upper bound (
5.1
) of Theorem
5.1
.
∎

5.3.
Proof of the lower bound

The aim of this section is to establish the lower bound (
5.1
)
of Theorem
5.1
.

Proof of (
5.1
).

Let us keep the notation used in the proof of the upper bound (
5.1
).
Recall that

\displaystyle I_{n}(x)

\displaystyle:=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\mathbb{E}f(t+S_{m})\mathbb{P}\left(x+S_{k}\in dt,\tau_{x}>k\right)

\displaystyle\qquad-\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\mathbb{E}\left(f(t+S_{m});\tau_{t}\leqslant m\right)\mathbb{P}\left(x+S_{k}\in dt,\tau_{x}>k\right)

(5.13)

\displaystyle=:I_{n,1}(x)-I_{n,2}(x).

Lower bound of
I_{n,1}(x)
.

By (
3.5
), we have

(5.14)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}f\left(t+S_{m}\right)

\displaystyle\geqslant\frac{1}{\sigma\sqrt{m}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big[h(s)-c\varepsilon f(s)\big]\phi\left(\frac{s-t}{\sigma\sqrt{m}}\right)ds-\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{1/2+\varepsilon}}\left\|f\right\|_{1}.

Proceeding in the same way as the proof of the upper bound (
5.1
)
(replacing the function
g
by
h-c\varepsilon f
and using the fact that
h\leqslant_{\varepsilon}f
),
one has

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\left[\frac{1}{\sigma\sqrt{m}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big[h(s)-c\varepsilon f(s)\big]\phi\left(\frac{s-t}{\sigma\sqrt{m}}\right)ds\right]\mathbb{P}\left(x+S_{k}\in dt,\tau_{x}>k\right)

\displaystyle\geqslant\frac{1}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big[h(s)-c\varepsilon f(s)\big]\psi\left(\frac{s}{\sigma\sqrt{n}},\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)ds

(5.15)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}h(s)\left[1-\Phi\left(\frac{s}{\sigma\varepsilon^{1/4}\sqrt{n}}\right)\right]ds-\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{1/2+\varepsilon}}\|f\|_{1}.

Combining (
5.14
) and (
5.3
),
we derive that for any
x\geqslant n^{1/2-\varepsilon}
,

\displaystyle I_{n,1}(x)

\displaystyle\geqslant\frac{1}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big[h(s)-c\varepsilon f(s)\big]\psi\left(\frac{s}{\sigma\sqrt{n}},\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)ds

(5.16)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}h(s)\left[1-\Phi\left(\frac{s}{\sigma\varepsilon^{1/4}\sqrt{n}}\right)\right]ds-\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{1/2+\varepsilon}}\|f\|_{1}.

Upper bound of
I_{n,2}(x)
.

It has been shown in (
3.5
) that

(5.17)

\displaystyle I_{n,2}(x)

\displaystyle=K_{1}+K_{2},

where
K_{1}
and
K_{2}
are given by (
3.5
).
For
K_{1}
, it is shown in (
3.5
) and (
3.5
) that

(5.18)

\displaystyle K_{1}

\displaystyle\leqslant\int_{\mathbb{R}}g(u)J(u)du+c_{\varepsilon}\frac{V(x)}{n^{1+\varepsilon}}\left\|g\right\|_{1},

where, with
F_{u}(t)=\frac{1}{\sigma\sqrt{m}}\phi(\frac{u-t}{\sigma\sqrt{m}})
,

\displaystyle J(u)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\varepsilon^{1/6}\sqrt{n}}F_{u}^{\prime}(t)\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{x+S_{k}}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\in\left[\frac{t}{\sigma\sqrt{k}},\frac{\varepsilon^{1/6}}{\sigma}\sqrt{\frac{n}{k}}\right],\tau_{x}>k\right)dt.

Using Theorem
2.8
, we get that for any
x\geqslant n^{1/2-\varepsilon}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{x+S_{k}}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\in\left[\frac{t}{\sigma\sqrt{k}},\frac{\varepsilon^{1/6}}{\sigma}\sqrt{\frac{n}{k}}\right],\tau_{x}>k\right)\leqslant G(t)+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon}}.

where

\displaystyle G(t)=\int_{\frac{t}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}}^{\frac{\varepsilon^{1/6}}{\sigma}\sqrt{\frac{n}{k}}}\psi\left(s,\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\right)ds.

Since
G(\varepsilon^{1/6}\sqrt{n})=0
and
F_{u}(0),G(0)\geqslant 0
,
it follows that

\displaystyle J(u)

\displaystyle\leqslant F_{u}(\varepsilon^{1/6}\sqrt{n})G(\varepsilon^{1/6}\sqrt{n})-F_{u}(0)G(0)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon^{1/6}\sqrt{n}}F_{u}(t)\psi\left(\frac{t}{\sigma\sqrt{k}},\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\right)dt+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon}}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon^{1/6}\sqrt{n}}|F_{u}^{\prime}(t)|dt

(5.19)

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon^{1/6}\sqrt{n}}F_{u}(t)\psi\left(\frac{t}{\sigma\sqrt{k}},\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\right)dt+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{1/2+\varepsilon}},

where in the last line we used the fact that
\int_{0}^{\varepsilon^{1/6}\sqrt{n}}|F_{u}^{\prime}(t)|dt\leqslant\frac{c}{\sqrt{m}}
.
By elementary calculations, we see that

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}(u):

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\varepsilon^{1/6}\sqrt{n}}F_{u}\left(t\right)\psi\left(\frac{t}{\sigma\sqrt{k}},\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\right)dt

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon^{1/6}\sqrt{n}}\frac{1}{\sigma\sqrt{m}}e^{-\frac{(u-t)^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}m}}\left(e^{-\frac{(t-x)^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}k}}-e^{-\frac{(t+x)^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}k}}\right)dt

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi}e^{-\frac{u^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}m}-\frac{x^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}k}}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon^{1/6}\sqrt{n}}\frac{1}{\sigma\sqrt{m}}e^{-\frac{nt^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}mk}+\frac{tu}{\sigma^{2}m}}e^{\frac{tx}{\sigma^{2}k}}\left(1-e^{-\frac{2tx}{\sigma^{2}k}}\right)dt.

Since
x\in[n^{1/2-\varepsilon},\eta\sqrt{n}]
and
t\in[0,\varepsilon^{1/6}\sqrt{n}]
,
we have
e^{\frac{tx}{\sigma^{2}k}}\leqslant c
for some constant
c>0
.
Using the fact that
e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}k}}\leqslant 1

and the inequality
1-e^{-t}\leqslant t
,
t\geqslant 0
, we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}(u)

\displaystyle\leqslant ce^{-\frac{u^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}m}}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon^{1/6}\sqrt{n}}\frac{t}{\sqrt{km}}e^{-\frac{nt^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}mk}+\frac{tu}{\sigma^{2}m}}dt

\displaystyle=ce^{-\frac{u^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}m}}e^{\frac{ku^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}mn}}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon^{1/6}\sqrt{n}}\frac{t}{\sqrt{km}}e^{-\frac{n}{2\sigma^{2}mk}(t-\frac{ku}{n})^{2}}dt

\displaystyle=ce^{-\frac{u^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}n}}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon^{1/6}\sqrt{n}}\frac{t}{\sqrt{km}}e^{-\frac{n}{2\sigma^{2}mk}(t-\frac{ku}{n})^{2}}dt.

By a change of variable
\frac{\sqrt{n}}{\sigma\sqrt{mk}}t=z
, it follows that

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}(u)

\displaystyle\leqslant ce^{-\frac{u^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}n}}\frac{\sqrt{km}}{n}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon^{1/6}n/\sqrt{\sigma^{2}mk}}ze^{-\frac{1}{2}(z-\frac{\sqrt{k}}{\sigma\sqrt{nm}}u)^{2}}dz

\displaystyle\leqslant ce^{-\frac{u^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}n}}\frac{\sqrt{km}}{n}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon^{1/6}n/\sqrt{\sigma^{2}mk}}zdz\leqslant c\varepsilon^{1/3}\frac{n}{\sqrt{km}}e^{-\frac{u^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}n}}\leqslant c\varepsilon^{1/12}e^{-\frac{u^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}n}}.

In view of (
5.3
), this implies that

\displaystyle J(u)

\displaystyle\leqslant c\frac{\varepsilon^{1/12}}{\sqrt{n}}e^{-\frac{u^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}n}}+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{1/2+\varepsilon}}.

Implementing this bound into (
5.18
), we obtain

(5.20)

\displaystyle K_{1}\leqslant c\frac{\varepsilon^{1/12}}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}g(t)e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}n}}dt+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{1/2+\varepsilon}}\|g\|_{1}.

We proceed to give an upper bound for
K_{2}
(cf. (
3.30
)).
Applying the upper bound (
5.1
) of Theorem
5.1
, we obtain

\displaystyle K_{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1+c\varepsilon}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}M(s)\psi\left(\frac{s}{\sigma\sqrt{k}},\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\right)ds

(5.21)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1+c\varepsilon}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}M(s)\left[1-\Phi\left(\frac{s}{\sigma\varepsilon^{1/4}\sqrt{k}}\right)\right]ds+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{1/2+\varepsilon}}\|M\|_{1},

where
s\mapsto M(s)
is defined by (
3.32
).
For the first term, applying the duality formula
((
3.4
) of Lemma
3.2
), we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}M(s)\psi\left(\frac{s}{\sigma\sqrt{k}},\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\right)ds

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\mathbb{E}\left(g(s+S_{m});\tau_{s-\varepsilon}\leqslant m\right)\mathds{1}_{\{s+\varepsilon>\varepsilon^{1/6}\sqrt{n}\}}\psi\left(\frac{s}{\sigma\sqrt{k}},\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\right)ds

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\mathbb{E}\left(g(t+\varepsilon+S_{m});\tau_{t}\leqslant m\right)\mathds{1}_{\{t+2\varepsilon>\varepsilon^{1/6}\sqrt{n}\}}\psi\left(\frac{t+\varepsilon}{\sigma\sqrt{k}},\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\right)dt

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}g(t+\varepsilon)\mathbb{E}\left[\psi\left(\frac{t+S_{m}^{*}+\varepsilon}{\sigma\sqrt{k}},\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\right);t+S_{m}^{*}+2\varepsilon>\varepsilon^{1/6}\sqrt{n},\tau_{t}^{*}\leqslant m\right]dt.

Since the function
(t,x)\mapsto\psi(t,x)
is bounded on
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}
, it follows that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}M(s)\psi\left(\frac{s}{\sigma\sqrt{k}},\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\right)ds

\displaystyle\leqslant c\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}g(t+\varepsilon)\mathbb{P}\left(t+S_{m}^{*}>\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon^{1/6}\sqrt{n},\tau_{t}^{*}\leqslant m\right)dt

(5.22)

\displaystyle\leqslant c\varepsilon^{1/12}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}g(t+\varepsilon)e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{16\sigma^{2}\sqrt{\varepsilon}n}}dt+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon}}\|g\|_{1},

where the last inequality holds due to the estimates of (
3.34
), (
3.35
) and (
3.5
).
Following the same proof of (
5.3
) and using the fact that
1-\Phi(\cdot)
is bounded on
\mathbb{R}
, one has

(5.23)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}M(s)\left[1-\Phi\left(\frac{s}{\sigma\varepsilon^{1/4}\sqrt{k}}\right)\right]ds\leqslant c\varepsilon^{1/12}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}g(t+\varepsilon)e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{16\sigma^{2}\sqrt{\varepsilon}n}}dt+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon}}\|g\|_{1}.

Implementing (
5.3
) and (
5.23
) into (
5.3
)
and using the fact that
\|M\|_{1}\leqslant\|g\|_{1}
, we get

\displaystyle K_{2}\leqslant c\frac{\varepsilon^{1/12}}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}g(t+\varepsilon)e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{16\sigma^{2}\sqrt{\varepsilon}n}}dt+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{1/2+\varepsilon}}\|g\|_{1}.

Combining this with (
5.17
) and (
5.20
), we derive that

\displaystyle I_{n,2}(x)

\displaystyle\leqslant c\frac{\varepsilon^{1/12}}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Big[g(t)+g(t+\varepsilon)\Big]e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}n}}dt+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{1/2+\varepsilon}}\|g\|_{1}.

This, together with (
5.3
),
finishes the proof of the lower bound (
5.1
). ∎

5.4.
Proof of Theorems
1.5
and
1.6

In this section we prove Theorems
1.5
and
1.6
by making use of
Theorem
5.1
.

Proof of Theorem
1.5
.

Without loss of generality, we assume that the target function
f
is non-negative.

Since
f\leqslant_{\varepsilon}\overline{f}_{\delta,\varepsilon}
with
\overline{f}_{\delta,\varepsilon}
defined by (
2.4
),
applying the upper bound (
5.1
) of Theorem
5.1
with
g=\overline{f}_{\delta,\varepsilon}
,
we derive that

\displaystyle I_{n}:

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left(f(x+S_{n}-y);\,\tau_{x}>n\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1+c\varepsilon}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\overline{f}_{\delta,\varepsilon}(t)\psi\left(\frac{t+y}{\sigma\sqrt{n}},\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)dt

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1+c\varepsilon}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\overline{f}_{\delta,\varepsilon}(t)\left[1-\Phi\left(\frac{t+y}{\sigma\varepsilon^{1/4}\sqrt{n}}\right)\right]dt+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{1/2+\varepsilon}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\overline{f}_{\delta,\varepsilon}(t)dt.

Since the function
\psi(\cdot,x)
is Lipschitz continuous on
\mathbb{R}
,
by elementary calculations, there exists a constant
c>0
such that for any
t\in\mathbb{R}
,

x\in[\eta^{-1}\sqrt{n},\eta\sqrt{n}]
and
y\in[\eta\sqrt{n},\sigma\sqrt{qn\log n}]
,

(5.24)

\displaystyle\left|\psi\left(\frac{t+y}{\sigma\sqrt{n}},\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)-\psi\left(\frac{y}{\sigma\sqrt{n}},\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)\right|\leqslant c\frac{|t|}{\sqrt{n}},

(5.25)

\displaystyle 1-\Phi\left(\frac{t+y}{\sigma\varepsilon^{1/4}\sqrt{n}}\right)\leqslant e^{-c/\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\psi\left(\frac{y}{\sigma\sqrt{n}},\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right),

(5.26)

\displaystyle\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon}}\leqslant\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon-q}}\psi\left(\frac{y}{\sigma\sqrt{n}},\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right),

which implies that

\displaystyle I_{n}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1+c\varepsilon}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\psi\left(\frac{y}{\sigma\sqrt{n}},\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)\left(1+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon-q}}\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\overline{f}_{\delta,\varepsilon}(t)dt+\frac{c}{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\overline{f}_{\delta,\varepsilon}(t)|t|dt.

Therefore, uniformly in
x\in[\eta^{-1}\sqrt{n},\eta\sqrt{n}]
and
y\in[\eta\sqrt{n},\sigma\sqrt{qn\log n}]
,

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mathbb{E}\left(f(x+S_{n}-y);\tau_{x}>n\right)}{\frac{1}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\psi\big(\frac{y}{\sigma\sqrt{n}},\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\big)}\leqslant(1+c\varepsilon)\int_{-\varepsilon}^{\infty}\overline{f}_{\delta,\varepsilon}(t)dt,

which proves the upper bound by
taking first
\varepsilon\to 0
and then
\delta\to 0
, and using Lemma
2.1
.
The proof of the lower bound can be carried out in the same way
using the lower bound (
5.1
) together with the fact that
there exists a constant
c>0
such that
uniformly in
x\in[\eta^{-1}\sqrt{n},\eta\sqrt{n}]
and
y\in[\eta\sqrt{n},\sigma\sqrt{qn\log n}]
,

\displaystyle\left|\phi\left(\frac{t+y}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)-\phi\left(\frac{y}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)\right|\leqslant c\frac{|t|}{\sqrt{n}},\quad\phi\left(\frac{y}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)\leqslant c\psi\left(\frac{y}{\sigma\sqrt{n}},\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right).

The proof of Theorem
1.5
is complete.
∎

Proof of Theorem
1.6
.

Using (
5.1
) of Theorem
5.1
with
f=\mathds{1}_{[y,y+\Delta]}

and
g=\mathds{1}_{[y-\varepsilon,y+\Delta+\varepsilon]}
,
we derive that uniformly in
x\in[\eta^{-1}\sqrt{n},\eta\sqrt{n}]
,

\displaystyle I_{n}:

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(x+S_{n}\in[0,\Delta]+y,\tau_{x}>n\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1+c\varepsilon}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\int_{-\varepsilon}^{\Delta+\varepsilon}\psi\left(\frac{t+y}{\sigma\sqrt{n}},\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)dt

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1+c\varepsilon}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\int_{-\varepsilon}^{\Delta+\varepsilon}\left[1-\Phi\left(\frac{t+y}{\sigma\varepsilon^{1/4}\sqrt{n}}\right)\right]dt+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{1/2+\varepsilon}}(\Delta+2\varepsilon).

Since the function
(s,x)\mapsto\psi(s,x)
is Lipschitz continuous on
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}
,
there exists a constant
c>0
such that for any
\Delta\in[\Delta_{0},n^{1/2-\varepsilon}]
,

t\in[-\varepsilon,\Delta+\varepsilon]
,
x\in[\eta^{-1}\sqrt{n},\eta\sqrt{n}]
and
y\in[\eta\sqrt{n},\sigma\sqrt{qn\log n}]
,

(5.27)

\displaystyle\left|\psi\left(\frac{t+y}{\sigma\sqrt{n}},\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)-\psi\left(\frac{y}{\sigma\sqrt{n}},\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)\right|\leqslant c\frac{t}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\leqslant\frac{c}{n^{\varepsilon}},

which, together with the fact that
\Delta+2\varepsilon\leqslant(1+c\varepsilon)\Delta
, implies that

\displaystyle\int_{-\varepsilon}^{\Delta+\varepsilon}\psi\left(\frac{t+y}{\sigma\sqrt{n}},\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)dt

\displaystyle\leqslant\Delta(1+c\varepsilon)\left[\psi\left(\frac{y}{\sigma\sqrt{n}},\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)+\frac{c}{n^{\varepsilon}}\right].

Similarly, using the inequality (
5.25
), we get

\displaystyle\int_{-\varepsilon}^{\Delta+\varepsilon}\left[1-\Phi\left(\frac{t+y}{\sigma\varepsilon^{1/4}\sqrt{n}}\right)\right]dt\leqslant\Delta(1+c\varepsilon)e^{-c/\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\psi\left(\frac{y}{\sigma\sqrt{n}},\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right).

Therefore, taking into account (
5.26
),
it follows that uniformly in
\Delta\in[\Delta_{0},n^{1/2-\varepsilon}]
,

x\in[\eta^{-1}\sqrt{n},\eta\sqrt{n}]
and
y\in[\eta\sqrt{n},\sigma\sqrt{qn\log n}]
,

\displaystyle I_{n}

\displaystyle\leqslant\Delta\frac{1+c\varepsilon}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\left[\psi\left(\frac{y}{\sigma\sqrt{n}},\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)+\frac{c}{n^{\varepsilon}}+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon}}\right]

(5.28)

\displaystyle\leqslant\Delta\frac{1+c\varepsilon}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\psi\left(\frac{y}{\sigma\sqrt{n}},\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)\left(1+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon-q}}\right),

which implies that
uniformly in
\Delta\in[\Delta_{0},n^{1/2-\varepsilon}]
,

x\in[\eta^{-1}\sqrt{n},\eta\sqrt{n}]
and
y\in[\eta\sqrt{n},\sigma\sqrt{qn\log n}]
,

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\frac{I_{n}}{\frac{\Delta}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\psi\left(\frac{y}{\sigma\sqrt{n}},\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)}\leqslant 1+c\varepsilon.

Since
\varepsilon>0
can be arbitrary small, this proves the upper bound.

For the lower bound, using (
5.1
)
with
f=\mathds{1}_{[y,y+\Delta]}
,
g=\mathds{1}_{[y-\varepsilon,y+\Delta+\varepsilon]}

and
h=\mathds{1}_{[y+\varepsilon,y+\Delta-\varepsilon]}
,
we get that uniformly in
x\in[\eta^{-1}\sqrt{n},\eta\sqrt{n}]
,

\displaystyle I_{n}

\displaystyle\geqslant\frac{1}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\int_{\varepsilon}^{\Delta-\varepsilon}\psi\left(\frac{t+y}{\sigma\sqrt{n}},\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)dt-\frac{c\varepsilon}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{0}^{\Delta}\psi\left(\frac{t+y}{\sigma\sqrt{n}},\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)dt

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{c}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{0}^{\Delta}\left[1-\Phi\left(\frac{t+y}{\sigma\varepsilon^{1/4}\sqrt{n}}\right)\right]dt-\frac{c\varepsilon^{1/12}}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{-2\varepsilon}^{\Delta+\varepsilon}\phi\left(\frac{t+y}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)dt

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{1/2+\varepsilon}}(\Delta+2\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=:I_{n,1}-I_{n,2}-I_{n,3}-I_{n,4}-I_{n,5}.

For
I_{n,1}
and
I_{n,2}
, using (
5.27
) and proceeding in the same way as (
5.4
),
we get that uniformly in
\Delta\in[\Delta_{0},n^{1/2-\varepsilon}]
,

\displaystyle I_{n,1}

\displaystyle\geqslant\Delta\frac{1-c\varepsilon}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\psi\left(\frac{y}{\sigma\sqrt{n}},\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)\left(1+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon-q}}\right),

\displaystyle I_{n,2}

\displaystyle\leqslant\Delta\frac{c\varepsilon}{\sqrt{n}}\psi\left(\frac{y}{\sigma\sqrt{n}},\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)\left(1+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon-q}}\right).

For
I_{n,3}
, using the inequality (
5.25
) yields that

\displaystyle I_{n,3}\leqslant\Delta\frac{ce^{-c/\sqrt{\varepsilon}}}{\sqrt{n}}\psi\left(\frac{y}{\sigma\sqrt{n}},\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right).

For
I_{n,4}
, by (
5.26
),
there exists a constant
c>0
such that for any
\Delta\in[\Delta_{0},n^{1/2-\varepsilon}]
,

t\in[-2\varepsilon,\Delta+\varepsilon]
,
x\in[\eta^{-1}\sqrt{n},\eta\sqrt{n}]
and
y\in[\eta\sqrt{n},\sigma\sqrt{qn\log n}]
,

\displaystyle\phi\left(\frac{t+y}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\phi\left(\frac{y}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)+\frac{c}{n^{\varepsilon}}\leqslant\psi\left(\frac{y}{\sigma\sqrt{n}},\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)\left(1+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon-q}}\right).

This, together with the fact that
\Delta+3\varepsilon\leqslant(1+c\varepsilon)\Delta
, implies that

\displaystyle I_{n,4}\leqslant\Delta\frac{c\varepsilon^{1/12}}{\sqrt{n}}\psi\left(\frac{y}{\sigma\sqrt{n}},\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)\left(1+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon-q}}\right).

For
I_{n,5}
, using again
\Delta+2\varepsilon\leqslant(1+c\varepsilon)\Delta
and (
5.26
), we derive that
uniformly in
\Delta\in[\Delta_{0},n^{1/2-\varepsilon}]
,

x\in[\eta^{-1}\sqrt{n},\eta\sqrt{n}]
and
y\in[\eta\sqrt{n},\sigma\sqrt{qn\log n}]
,

\displaystyle I_{n,5}

\displaystyle\leqslant\Delta\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{1/2+\varepsilon}}\leqslant\Delta\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{1/2+\varepsilon-q}}\psi\left(\frac{y}{\sigma\sqrt{n}},\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right).

Putting together the above bounds for
I_{n,1}
,
I_{n,2}
,
I_{n,3}
,
I_{n,4}
and
I_{n,5}
,
letting first
n\to\infty
and then
\varepsilon\to 0
, we obtain the desired lower bound.
The proof of Theorem
1.6
is complete.
∎

6.
Conditioned local limit theorem far from the boundary

The goal of this section is to establish
Theorems
1.7
,
1.8
,
1.9
,
1.10
,

1.11
and
1.12
.
Recall that
\phi^{+}_{v}(s)=\frac{s}{v}e^{-\frac{s^{2}}{2v}}\mathds{1}_{\{s\geqslant 0\}}
,
s\in\mathbb{R}

is the Rayleigh density with scale parameter
\sqrt{v}
,
and
\psi_{v}
is defined by (
5.3
).
The following lemma will be used to prove Theorems
1.7
and
1.8
.

Lemma 6.1
.

For any
x\geqslant 0
and
v\in(0,1)
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\phi^{+}_{v}(s)\psi_{1-v}\left(s,x\right)ds=\sqrt{v}\phi^{+}(x).

Proof.

Notice that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\phi^{+}_{v}(s)\psi_{1-v}\left(s,x\right)ds

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi(1-v)}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\frac{s}{v}e^{-\frac{s^{2}}{2v}}\left(e^{-\frac{(s-x)^{2}}{2(1-v)}}-e^{-\frac{(s+x)^{2}}{2(1-v)}}\right)ds

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{v\sqrt{2\pi(1-v)}}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{2(1-v)}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}se^{-\frac{s^{2}}{2v(1-v)}}\left(e^{\frac{sx}{1-v}}-e^{-\frac{sx}{1-v}}\right)ds.

For the first integral, we have that for any
x\geqslant 0
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}se^{-\frac{s^{2}}{2v(1-v)}+\frac{sx}{1-v}}ds=e^{\frac{vx^{2}}{2(1-v)}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}se^{-\frac{(s-vx)^{2}}{2v(1-v)}}ds

\displaystyle=e^{\frac{vx^{2}}{2(1-v)}}\int_{-vx}^{\infty}\left(t+vx\right)e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{2v(1-v)}}dt

(6.1)

\displaystyle=e^{\frac{vx^{2}}{2(1-v)}}\left[v(1-v)e^{-\frac{vx^{2}}{2(1-v)}}+vx\int_{-vx}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{2v(1-v)}}dt\right].

For the second integral, by replacing
x
with
-x
, we get

(6.2)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}se^{-\frac{s^{2}}{2v(1-v)}-\frac{sx}{1-v}}ds=e^{\frac{vx^{2}}{2(1-v)}}\left[v(1-v)e^{-\frac{vx^{2}}{2(1-v)}}-vx\int_{vx}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{2v(1-v)}}dt\right].

Therefore, taking the difference between (
6
) and (
6.2
), we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\phi^{+}_{v}(s)\psi_{1-v}\left(s,x\right)ds

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{v\sqrt{2\pi(1-v)}}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{2}}\left(vx\int_{-vx}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{2v(1-v)}}dt+vx\int_{vx}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{2v(1-v)}}dt\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi(1-v)}}xe^{-\frac{x^{2}}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{2v(1-v)}}dt=\sqrt{v}\phi^{+}(x),

which ends the proof of the lemma.
∎

6.1.
A non-asymptotic conditioned local limit theorem

Theorem 6.2
.

Assume
1
and
2
.
Then, for any
\eta>0
, there exist constants
c>0
and
\varepsilon_{0}>0
such that for any
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})

and any sequence of positive numbers
(\alpha_{n})_{n\geqslant 1}
satisfying
\lim_{n\to\infty}\alpha_{n}=0
,
one can find a constant
c_{\varepsilon}>0
such that
uniformly in
x\in[n^{1/2-\varepsilon},\eta\sqrt{n}]
and
n\geqslant 1
, the following holds:

1. For any measurable functions
f,g:\mathbb{R}\mapsto\mathbb{R}_{+}
satisfying
f\leqslant_{\varepsilon}g
and

\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}g(t-\varepsilon)(1+t)dt<\infty
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(f(x+S_{n});\tau_{x}>n\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{2}{\sigma^{2}\sqrt{2\pi}n}\left[\phi^{+}\left(\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)+\left(c\varepsilon^{1/4}+c_{\varepsilon}\alpha_{n}\right)\right]\int_{0}^{\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}}g(t-\varepsilon)V^{*}(t)dt

(6.3)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{c}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}}^{\infty}g(t-\varepsilon)dt+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{1+\varepsilon}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}g(t-\varepsilon)(1+t)dt.

2. For any measurable functions
f,g,h:\mathbb{R}\mapsto\mathbb{R}_{+}

satisfying
h\leqslant_{\varepsilon}f\leqslant_{\varepsilon}g
and
\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}g(t-\varepsilon)(1+t)dt<\infty
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(f\left(x+S_{n}\right);\tau_{x}>n\right)

\displaystyle\geqslant\frac{2}{\sigma^{2}\sqrt{2\pi}n}\left[\phi^{+}\left(\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)-\left(c\varepsilon+c_{\varepsilon}\alpha_{n}\right)\right]\int_{0}^{\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}}h(t+\varepsilon)V^{*}(t)dt

(6.4)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{c}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}}^{\infty}g(t-\varepsilon)dt-\left(\frac{c\varepsilon^{1/12}}{n}+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{1+\varepsilon}}\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}g(t-\varepsilon)(1+t)dt.

Proof.

We first prove the upper bound (
6.2
).
Set
m=\left[n/2\right]
and
k=n-m.

It is shown in (
4.3
), (
4.4
) and (
4.5
) that

\displaystyle I_{n}(x):=\mathbb{E}\left(f(x+S_{n});\tau_{x}>n\right)=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}I_{m}(t)\mathbb{P}\left(x+S_{k}\in dt,\tau_{x}>k\right)

and
I_{m}\leqslant_{\varepsilon}H_{m}
, where

\displaystyle H_{m}(t):=\mathbb{E}\left(g\left(t+S_{m}\right);\tau_{t+\varepsilon}>m\right)\mathds{1}_{\{t\geqslant-\varepsilon\}},\quad t\in\mathbb{R}.

By the upper bound (
5.1
) of Theorem
5.1
,
we get that uniformly in
x\in[n^{1/2-\varepsilon},\eta\sqrt{n}]
,

(6.5)

\displaystyle I_{n}(x)\leqslant(1+c\varepsilon)\left(J_{1}+J_{2}+J_{3}\right),

where

\displaystyle J_{1}=\frac{1}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}H_{m}(t)\psi\left(\frac{t}{\sigma\sqrt{k}},\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\right)dt,

\displaystyle J_{2}=\frac{1}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}H_{m}(t)\left[1-\Phi\left(\frac{t}{\sigma\varepsilon^{1/4}\sqrt{k}}\right)\right]dt,\quad J_{3}=\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{1/2+\varepsilon}}\left\|H_{m}\right\|_{1}.

Bound of
J_{1}
.

As in (
4.1
), (
4.8
) and (
4.9
), using a change of variable
and the duality formula (Lemma
3.2
), we get

\displaystyle J_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\int_{-\varepsilon}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}(g(t+S_{m});\tau_{t+\varepsilon}>m)\psi\left(\frac{t}{\sigma\sqrt{k}},\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\right)dt

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\mathbb{E}(g(t+S_{m}-\varepsilon);\tau_{t}>m)\psi\left(\frac{t-\varepsilon}{\sigma\sqrt{k}},\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\right)dt

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}g(t-\varepsilon)\mathbb{E}\left[\psi\left(\frac{t+S_{m}^{*}-\varepsilon}{\sigma\sqrt{k}},\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\right);\tau_{t}^{*}>m\right]dt

(6.6)

\displaystyle=:J_{11}+J_{12},

where

\displaystyle J_{11}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\int_{\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}}^{\infty}g(t-\varepsilon)\mathbb{E}\left[\psi\left(\frac{t+S_{m}^{*}-\varepsilon}{\sigma\sqrt{k}},\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\right);\tau_{t}^{*}>m\right]dt,

\displaystyle J_{12}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\int_{0}^{\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}}g(t-\varepsilon)\mathbb{E}\left[\psi\left(\frac{t+S_{m}^{*}-\varepsilon}{\sigma\sqrt{k}},\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\right);\tau_{t}^{*}>m\right]dt.

Bound of
J_{11}
.

Since the function
\psi(\cdot,\cdot)
is bounded on
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}
,
we get

(6.7)

\displaystyle J_{11}\leqslant\frac{c}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}}^{\infty}g(t-\varepsilon)dt.

Bound of
J_{12}
.

For brevity we denote
\psi^{\prime}_{1}(u,x)=\frac{d}{du}\psi(u,x)
.
Following the proof of (
4.1
) and (
4.1
),
from the conditioned integral limit theorem (
2.26
) of Corollary
2.9

and the fact that
\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|\psi^{\prime}_{1}(u,\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{k}})|du<\infty
,
one has that uniformly in
t\in[0,\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}]
,

\displaystyle\bigg|

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\psi\left(\frac{t+S_{m}^{*}-\varepsilon}{\sigma\sqrt{k}},\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\right);\tau_{t}^{*}>m)\right]

\displaystyle-\frac{2V^{*}(t)}{\sigma\sqrt{2\pi m}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\psi^{\prime}_{1}\left(u,\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\right)\left(1-\Phi^{+}(u_{k,m,\varepsilon})\right)du\bigg|\leqslant c_{\varepsilon}\left(\alpha_{n}+n^{-\varepsilon}\right)\frac{V^{*}(t)}{n^{1/2}},

where
u_{k,m,\varepsilon}=\frac{u\sigma\sqrt{k}+\varepsilon}{\sigma\sqrt{m}}
.
Using integration by parts and the fact that the function
(\phi^{+})^{\prime}
is Lipschitz on
\mathbb{R}_{+}

and
\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\psi(u,\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{k}})du<\infty
uniformly in
x\in[n^{1/2-\varepsilon},\eta\sqrt{n}]
,
we get

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\psi^{\prime}_{1}\left(u,\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\right)\left(1-\Phi^{+}(u_{k,m,\varepsilon})\right)du

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{k}{m}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\psi\left(u,\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\right)\phi^{+}(u_{k,m,\varepsilon})du

\displaystyle\leqslant\sqrt{\frac{k}{m}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\psi\left(u,\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\right)\phi^{+}\left(u\frac{\sqrt{k}}{\sqrt{m}}\right)du+\frac{c\varepsilon}{\sqrt{n}}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{n}{m}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\psi\left(\frac{s}{\sqrt{k/n}},\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\right)\phi^{+}\left(\frac{s}{\sqrt{m/n}}\right)ds+\frac{c\varepsilon}{\sqrt{n}}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{k}{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\psi_{\frac{k}{n}}\left(s,\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)\phi^{+}_{\frac{m}{n}}(s)ds+\frac{c\varepsilon}{\sqrt{n}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{km}}{n}\phi^{+}\left(\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)+\frac{c\varepsilon}{\sqrt{n}},

where in the last equality we used Lemma
6.1
.
Thus, we deduce that

(6.8)

\displaystyle J_{12}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{2}{\sigma^{2}\sqrt{2\pi}n}\left[\phi^{+}\left(\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)+c_{\varepsilon}\left(\alpha_{n}+n^{-\varepsilon}\right)\right]\int_{0}^{\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}}g(t-\varepsilon)V^{*}(t)dt.

Bound of
J_{2}
.
In the same way as in (
6.1
),
using a change of variable
and the duality formula ((
3.3
) of Lemma
3.2
), one has

(6.9)

\displaystyle J_{2}=\frac{1}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}g(t-\varepsilon)\mathbb{E}\left[1-\Phi\left(\frac{t+S_{m}^{*}-\varepsilon}{\sigma\varepsilon^{1/4}\sqrt{k}}\right);\tau_{t}^{*}>m\right]dt.

We decompose
J_{2}
into two parts:
J_{2}=J_{21}+J_{22}
, where

\displaystyle J_{21}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\int_{\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}}^{\infty}g(t-\varepsilon)\mathbb{E}\left[1-\Phi\left(\frac{t+S_{m}^{*}-\varepsilon}{\sigma\varepsilon^{1/4}\sqrt{k}}\right);\tau_{t}^{*}>m\right]dt,

\displaystyle J_{22}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\int_{0}^{\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}}g(t-\varepsilon)\mathbb{E}\left[1-\Phi\left(\frac{t+S_{m}^{*}-\varepsilon}{\sigma\varepsilon^{1/4}\sqrt{k}}\right);\tau_{t}^{*}>m\right]dt.

Bound of
J_{21}
.

Since the function
1-\Phi
is bounded,
we get

(6.10)

\displaystyle J_{21}\leqslant\frac{c}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}}^{\infty}g(t-\varepsilon)dt.

Bound of
J_{22}
.

Proceeding in the same way as in (
4.1
) and (
4.1
),
from the conditioned integral limit theorem (
2.26
) of Corollary
2.9

and the fact that
1-\Phi(\infty)=0
,
one has that uniformly in
t\in[0,\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}]
,

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}\left[1-\Phi\left(\frac{t+S_{m}^{*}-\varepsilon}{\sigma\varepsilon^{1/4}\sqrt{k}}\right);\tau_{t}^{*}>m\right]-\frac{2V^{*}(t)}{\sigma\sqrt{2\pi m}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\phi(v)\Phi^{+}(v_{k,m,\varepsilon})dv\right|

(6.11)

\displaystyle\leqslant c_{\varepsilon}\left(\alpha_{n}+n^{-\varepsilon}\right)\frac{V^{*}(t)}{n^{1/2}},

where
v_{k,m,\varepsilon}=\frac{\varepsilon^{1/4}\sigma\sqrt{k}v+\varepsilon}{\sigma\sqrt{m}}
.
Since the function
\Phi^{+}
is Lipschitz continuous, by elementary calculations, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\phi(v)\Phi^{+}(v_{k,m,\varepsilon})dv

\displaystyle\leqslant\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\phi(v)\Phi^{+}\left(\varepsilon^{1/4}\frac{\sqrt{k}}{\sqrt{m}}v\right)dv+\frac{c\varepsilon}{\sqrt{n}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\left(1+\frac{\sqrt{\varepsilon}k}{m}\right)^{-1/2}+\frac{c\varepsilon}{\sqrt{n}}\leqslant c\varepsilon^{1/4}.

Combining this with (
6.1
) and (
6.10
), we derive that

\displaystyle J_{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{n}\left(c\varepsilon^{1/4}+c_{\varepsilon}\alpha_{n}+c_{\varepsilon}n^{-\varepsilon}\right)\int_{0}^{\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}}g(t-\varepsilon)V^{*}(t)dt

(6.12)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{c}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}}^{\infty}g(t-\varepsilon)dt.

Bound of
J_{3}
.

By (
4.17
), we have

(6.13)

\displaystyle J_{3}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{1+\varepsilon}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}g(t-\varepsilon)(1+t)dt.

Putting together (
6.7
), (
6.8
), (
6.1
)
and (
6.13
),
we conclude the proof of the upper bound (
6.2
) of the theorem.

We next sketch the proof of the lower bound (
6.2
).
Similarly to the proof of (
6.5
), we use the lower bound (
5.1
) of Theorem
5.1
to get

I_{n}(x)\geqslant K_{1}-K_{2}-K_{3}
, where

\displaystyle K_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}L_{m}(t)\psi\left(\frac{t}{\sigma\sqrt{k}},\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\right)dt,

\displaystyle K_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}H_{m}(t)\left[1-\Phi\left(\frac{t}{\sigma\varepsilon^{1/4}\sqrt{k}}\right)\right]dt

\displaystyle K_{3}

\displaystyle=\frac{c\varepsilon}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}H_{m}(t)\psi\left(\frac{t}{\sigma\sqrt{k}},\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\right)dt

\displaystyle\quad+c\frac{\varepsilon^{1/12}}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big[H_{m}(t)+H_{m}(t+\varepsilon)\big]\phi\left(\frac{t}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\right)dt+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{1/2+\varepsilon}}\left\|H_{m}\right\|_{1},

with
H_{m}
and
L_{m}
defined by (
4.5
