) and (
4.20
), respectively.

Bound of
K_{1}
.

Note that
L_{m}(t)=0
for
t<\varepsilon
. As in the estimate of
J_{1}
(cf. (
6.1
)),
using the duality formula (Lemma
3.2
), we get

\displaystyle K_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}h(t+\varepsilon)\mathbb{E}\left[\psi\left(\frac{t+S_{m}^{*}+\varepsilon}{\sigma\sqrt{k}},\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\right);\tau_{t}^{*}>m\right]dt

\displaystyle\geqslant\frac{1}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\int_{0}^{\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}}h(t+\varepsilon)\mathbb{E}\left[\psi\left(\frac{t+S_{m}^{*}+\varepsilon}{\sigma\sqrt{k}},\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\right);\tau_{t}^{*}>m\right]dt,

where in the last inequality we used the fact that
t+S_{m}^{*}+\varepsilon\geqslant\varepsilon
on the event
\tau_{t}^{*}>m
,
so that
\psi>0
.
Following the proof of (
6.8
), one has

\displaystyle K_{1}\geqslant\frac{2}{\sigma^{2}\sqrt{2\pi}n}\left[\phi^{+}\left(\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)-c_{\varepsilon}\left(\alpha_{n}+n^{-\varepsilon}\right)\right]\int_{0}^{\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}}h(t+\varepsilon)V^{*}(t)dt.

Bound of
K_{2}
.

By (
6.9
) and (
6.1
), we have

\displaystyle K_{2}=\frac{1}{\sigma\sqrt{k}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}g(t-\varepsilon)\mathbb{E}\left[1-\Phi\left(\frac{t+S_{m}^{*}-\varepsilon}{\sigma\varepsilon^{1/4}\sqrt{k}}\right);\tau_{t}^{*}>m\right]dt

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{n}\left(c\varepsilon^{1/4}+c_{\varepsilon}\alpha_{n}+c_{\varepsilon}n^{-\varepsilon}\right)\int_{0}^{\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}}g(t-\varepsilon)V^{*}(t)dt+\frac{c}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}}^{\infty}g(t-\varepsilon)dt.

Bound of
K_{3}
.

Since the functions
\psi
and
\phi
are bounded, using (
4.17
) we get

\displaystyle K_{3}\leqslant\left(\frac{c\varepsilon^{1/12}}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{1/2+\varepsilon}}\right)\|H_{m}\|_{1}\leqslant\left(\frac{c\varepsilon^{1/12}}{n}+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{1+\varepsilon}}\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}g(t-\varepsilon)(1+t)dt.

Collecting the above bounds for
K_{1}
,
K_{2}
and
K_{3}
,
and using the fact that
V^{*}(t)\leqslant c(1+t)
for
t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
and
h(t+\varepsilon)\leqslant f(t)\leqslant g(t-\varepsilon)
for any
t\in\mathbb{R}
,
we conclude the proof of the lower bound (
6.2
).
∎

6.2.
Proof of Theorems
1.7
and
1.8

Proof of Theorem
1.7
.

We first prove (
1.16
).
Since
\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}f(t)(1+t)^{\gamma}dt<\infty
for some constant
\gamma>1
,
the result (
1.16
) is a consequence of Theorem
6.2
,
Lemma
2.1
and the Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem.

We next prove (
1.17
).
Applying the upper bound (
6.2
) of Theorem
6.2

with
g=\overline{f}_{\delta,\varepsilon}
and
\alpha_{n}
replaced by
2\alpha_{n}
,
we get that uniformly in
x\in[\eta^{-1}\sqrt{n},\eta\sqrt{n}]
and
y\in[\alpha_{n}^{-1},\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(f(x+S_{n}-y);\tau_{x}>n\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{2}{\sigma^{2}\sqrt{2\pi}n}\left[\phi^{+}\left(\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)+\big(c\varepsilon^{1/4}+c_{\varepsilon}\alpha_{n}\big)\right]\int_{0}^{2\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}}\overline{f}_{\delta,\varepsilon}(t-y-\varepsilon)V^{*}(t)dt

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{c}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{2\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}}^{\infty}\overline{f}_{\delta,\varepsilon}(t-y-\varepsilon)dt+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{1+\varepsilon}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\overline{f}_{\delta,\varepsilon}(t-y-\varepsilon)(1+t)dt.

Using (
4.22
), (
4.23
) and (
4.24
),
and the fact that
\phi^{+}(\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}})
is bounded from below by a constant
c_{\eta}>0

for
x\in[\eta^{-1}\sqrt{n},\eta\sqrt{n}]
, we get
that uniformly in
x\in[\eta^{-1}\sqrt{n},\eta\sqrt{n}]
and
y\in[\alpha_{n}^{-1},\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}]
,

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mathbb{E}\left(f(x+S_{n}-y);\tau_{x}>n\right)}{\frac{2y}{\sqrt{2\pi}\sigma^{2}n}\phi^{+}(\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}})}\leqslant\left(1+c\varepsilon^{1/4}\right)\int_{-\varepsilon}^{\infty}\overline{f}_{\delta,\varepsilon}(u)du.

This yields the desired upper bound by taking first
\varepsilon\to 0
and then
\delta\to 0
, and using Lemma
2.1
.
The lower bound can be proved in the same way
by using (
6.2
) of Theorem
6.2
.
The proof of (
1.17
) is complete.
∎

Proof of Theorem
1.8
.

Applying (
6.2
) of Theorem
6.2

with
f=\mathds{1}_{[y,y+\Delta]}
,
g=\mathds{1}_{[y-\varepsilon,y+\Delta+\varepsilon]}

and
\alpha_{n}
replaced by
2\alpha_{n}
(so that the second integral in (
6.2
) vanishes),
we derive that uniformly in
x\in[\eta^{-1}\sqrt{n},\eta\sqrt{n}]
,
y\in[\alpha_{n}^{-1},\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}]

and
\Delta\in[\Delta_{0},o(y)]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(x+S_{n}\in[0,\Delta]+y,\,\tau_{x}>n\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{2}{\sigma^{2}\sqrt{2\pi}n}\left[\phi^{+}\left(\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)+\big(c\varepsilon^{1/4}+c_{\varepsilon}\alpha_{n}\big)\right]\int_{-\varepsilon}^{\Delta+\varepsilon}V^{*}(t+y+\varepsilon)dt

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{1+\varepsilon}}\int_{-\varepsilon}^{\Delta+\varepsilon}(1+t+y)dt.

Since
1\leqslant c_{\eta}\phi^{+}(\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}})

holds uniformly in
x\in[\eta^{-1}\sqrt{n},\eta\sqrt{n}]
for some constant
c_{\eta}>0
,
using (
4.2
) and (
4.27
),
we obtain that uniformly in
x\in[\eta^{-1}\sqrt{n},\eta\sqrt{n}]
,
y\in[\alpha_{n}^{-1},\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}]

and
\Delta\in[\Delta_{0},o(y)]
,

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mathbb{P}\left(x+S_{n}\in[0,\Delta]+y,\,\tau_{x}>n\right)}{\frac{2y\Delta}{\sigma^{2}\sqrt{2\pi}n}\phi^{+}\big(\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\big)}\leqslant\left(1+c_{\eta}\varepsilon^{1/4}\right),

which concludes the proof of the upper bound by letting
\varepsilon\to 0
.
The lower bound can be obtained in a similar way by using (
6.2
).
The proof of Theorem
1.8
is complete.
∎

6.3.
Proof of Theorems
1.9
and
1.10

Theorems
1.9
and
1.10
are easy consequences of the following result,
which is deduced from Theorems
1.3
and
1.7
.

Theorem 6.3
.

Assume
1
and
3
.
Let
f:\mathbb{R}_{+}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
be a directly Riemann integrable function
satisfying
\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}f(t)e^{-\lambda t}(1+t)^{\gamma}dt<\infty
for some constant
\gamma>1
.
Then, for any sequence of positive numbers
(\alpha_{n})_{n\geqslant 1}
satisfying

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\alpha_{n}=0
, we have,
as
n\to\infty
, uniformly in
x\in[0,\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}]
,

(6.14)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(f(x+S_{n});\,\tau_{x}>n\right)\sim\frac{2V_{\lambda}(x)e^{n\Lambda(\lambda)+\lambda x}}{\sqrt{2\pi}\sigma_{\lambda}^{3}n^{3/2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}f(t)e^{-\lambda t}V_{\lambda}^{\ast}(t)dt.

Moreover, for any
\eta\geqslant 1
,
we have, as
n\to\infty
, uniformly in
x\in[\eta^{-1}\sqrt{n},\eta\sqrt{n}]
,

(6.15)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(f(x+S_{n});\,\tau_{x}>n\right)\sim\frac{2e^{n\Lambda(\lambda)+\lambda x}}{\sqrt{2\pi}\sigma_{\lambda}^{2}n}\phi^{+}\left(\frac{x}{\sigma_{\lambda}\sqrt{n}}\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}f(t)e^{-\lambda t}V_{\lambda}^{\ast}(t)dt.

Proof.

By a change of measure, we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(f(x+S_{n});\tau_{x}>n\right)

\displaystyle=e^{n\Lambda(\lambda)}\mathbb{E}_{\lambda}\left(e^{-\lambda S_{n}}f(x+S_{n});\tau_{x}>n\right)

\displaystyle=e^{n\Lambda(\lambda)+\lambda x}\mathbb{E}_{\lambda}\left(e^{-\lambda(x+S_{n})}f(x+S_{n});\tau_{x}>n\right).

Since
\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}f(t)e^{-\lambda t}(1+t)^{\gamma}dt<\infty
for some constant
\gamma>1
,
applying (
1.9
) of Theorem
1.3
we get (
6.14
),
and applying (
1.16
) of Theorem
1.7
we obtain (
6.15
).
∎

Proof of Theorems
1.9
and
1.10
.

In the particular case when the random walk
S_{n}
has negative drift, i.e.
\mu=\mathbb{E}X_{1}<0

(in this case
\lambda>0
by condition
3
),
taking
f=\mathds{1}_{[0,\infty)}
in (
6.14
) we get Theorem
1.9
,
and taking
f=\mathds{1}_{[0,\infty)}
in (
6.15
) we get Theorem
1.10
.
∎

6.4.
Proof of Theorems
1.11
and
1.12

To prove Theorems
1.11
and
1.12
, we need the following lemma.

Lemma 6.4
.

Assume
3
for some
\lambda>0
. Then,

(6.16)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\mathbb{P}(t+X_{1}<0)e^{-\lambda t}V_{\lambda}^{\ast}(t)dt=\mathbb{E}e^{\lambda X_{1}}\int_{-\infty}^{0}e^{-\lambda t}V_{\lambda}^{\ast}(t)dt\in(0,\infty).

Proof.

By Fubini’s theorem and a change of variable, we get

\displaystyle I:

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\mathbb{P}(t+X_{1}<0)e^{-\lambda t}V_{\lambda}^{\ast}(t)dt

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathds{1}_{\{t+X_{1}<0\}}e^{-\lambda t}V_{\lambda}^{\ast}(t)dt

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\int_{X_{1}}^{0}e^{-\lambda(s-X_{1})}V_{\lambda}^{\ast}(s-X_{1})ds

\displaystyle=\int_{-\infty}^{0}\mathbb{E}\left[e^{-\lambda(s-X_{1})}V_{\lambda}^{\ast}(s-X_{1});s-X_{1}\geqslant 0\right]ds.

By the harmonicity property of the function
V_{\lambda}^{\ast}
, we have that for any
s\leqslant 0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\lambda}\left[V_{\lambda}^{\ast}(s-X_{1});s-X_{1}\geqslant 0\right]=V_{\lambda}^{\ast}(s).

Then, by the definition of
\mathbb{E}_{\lambda}
it follows that

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}e^{\lambda X_{1}}\int_{-\infty}^{0}e^{-\lambda s}\mathbb{E}_{\lambda}\left[V_{\lambda}^{\ast}(s-X_{1});s-X_{1}\geqslant 0\right]ds

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}e^{\lambda X_{1}}\int_{-\infty}^{0}e^{-\lambda s}V_{\lambda}^{\ast}(s)ds,

which proves the equality in (
6.16
).
Using Markov’s inequality, condition
3
and the fact that
V_{\lambda}^{\ast}

is strictly positive and non-decresing on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
,
it is easy to see that
I\in[0,\infty)
.
Suppose that
I=0
, then
\mathbb{P}(X_{1}<0)=0
. This contradicts to the first requirement in condition
3
,
so that
I\in(0,\infty)
.
∎

Proof of Theorems
1.11
and
1.12
.

We only give a proof of Theorem
1.12
since Theorem
1.11

is a particular case of Theorem
1.12
by taking
\lambda=0
.

We first prove (
1.25
).
By the definition of
\tau_{x}
, we have for any
n\geqslant 1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\tau_{x}=n+1\right)=\mathbb{P}\left(x+S_{n+1}<0,\tau_{x}>n\right).

From the Markov property, it follows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(x+S_{n+1}<0,\tau_{x}>n\right)=\mathbb{E}\left(f(x+S_{n});\tau_{x}>n\right),

where

\displaystyle f(t)=\mathbb{P}(t+X_{1}<0)\mathds{1}_{\{t\geqslant 0\}},\quad t\in\mathbb{R}.

It is easy to see that
0\leqslant f\leqslant 1
and
f
is a non-increasing function on
\mathbb{R}
.
In addition, using Markov’s inequality and condition
3
, we get that for
t\geqslant 1
,

\displaystyle f(t)\leqslant\mathbb{P}(|X_{1}|>t)\leqslant\frac{1}{t^{2+\delta}e^{\lambda t}}\mathbb{E}|X_{1}|^{2+\delta}e^{\lambda X_{1}}\leqslant\frac{c}{t^{2+\delta}e^{\lambda t}},

so that by taking
\gamma=1+\frac{\delta}{2}
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}f(t)e^{-\lambda t}(1+t)^{\gamma}dt

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}f(t)e^{-\lambda t}(1+t)^{\gamma}dt+\int_{1}^{\infty}f(t)e^{-\lambda t}(1+t)^{\gamma}dt\leqslant c.

Hence the function
f
satisfies the condition stated in Theorem
6.3
.
Using (
6.14
) of Theorem
6.3
,
we obtain that as
n\to\infty
, uniformly in
x\in[0,\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\tau_{x}=n+1\right)\sim\frac{2V_{\lambda}(x)e^{n\Lambda(\lambda)+\lambda x}}{\sqrt{2\pi}\sigma_{\lambda}^{3}n^{3/2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\mathbb{P}(t+X_{1}<0)e^{-\lambda t}V_{\lambda}^{\ast}(t)dt,

which, together with Lemma
6.4
,
concludes the proof of (
1.25
).

The asymptotic (
1.26
) can be obtained in the same way
by using (
6.15
) of Theorem
6.3
.
The proof of Theorems
1.11
and
1.12
is complete.
∎

7.
Appendix: Proof of conditioned integral limit theorems

7.1.
Auxiliary results

The goal of this section is to prove some auxiliary results which will be used to
establish the conditioned integral limit theorems (Theorems
2.7
and
2.8
).

Lemma 7.1
.

Assume
2
for some
\delta>0
.
Then there exists a constant
c>0
such that
for any
\varepsilon\in[0,\frac{\delta}{2(2+\delta)}]
,
n\geqslant 1
and
x\geqslant n^{1/2-\varepsilon}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(x+S_{n};\tau_{x}>n\right)\leqslant x+cn^{1/2-\varepsilon}\leqslant\left(1+\frac{c}{n^{\varepsilon}}\right)x.

Proof.

By the optional stopping theorem, we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(x+S_{n};\tau_{x}>n)

\displaystyle=x-\mathbb{E}(x+S_{n};\tau_{x}\leqslant n)

(7.1)

\displaystyle=x-\mathbb{E}(x+S_{\tau_{x}};\tau_{x}\leqslant n).

By the definition of
\tau_{x}
, we have
X_{\tau_{x}}\leqslant x+S_{\tau_{x}}<0
and hence

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(x+S_{n};\tau_{x}>n)<x+\mathbb{E}(|X_{\tau_{x}}|;\tau_{x}\leqslant n).

Using Hölder’s inequality, condition
2
and Markov’s inequality gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(|X_{k}|;|X_{k}|>n^{1/2-\varepsilon}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\mathbb{E}^{\frac{1}{2+\delta}}\left(|X_{k}|^{2+\delta}\right)\mathbb{P}^{\frac{1+\delta}{2+\delta}}\left(|X_{k}|>n^{1/2-\varepsilon}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant cn^{-(1/2-\varepsilon)(1+\delta)},

from which it follows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(|X_{\tau_{x}}|;\tau_{x}\leqslant n\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant n^{1/2-\varepsilon}+\mathbb{E}\left(|X_{\tau_{x}}|;|X_{\tau_{x}}|>n^{1/2-\varepsilon},\tau_{x}\leqslant n\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant n^{1/2-\varepsilon}+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left(|X_{k}|;|X_{k}|>n^{1/2-\varepsilon}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant n^{1/2-\varepsilon}+cn^{1-(1/2-\varepsilon)(1+\delta)}\leqslant cn^{1/2-\varepsilon},

where in the last inequality we used the fact that
\varepsilon\in[0,\frac{\delta}{2(2+\delta)}]
.
The desired result follows. ∎

For
\varepsilon\in(0,1/2)
and
x\geqslant 0
, consider the first time
when the absolute value of the random walk
(x+S_{k})_{k\geqslant 1}
exceeds the level
n^{1/2-\varepsilon}
:

(7.2)

\displaystyle\nu_{n}=\nu_{n,x,\varepsilon}=\inf\left\{k\geqslant 1:|x+S_{k}|>n^{1/2-\varepsilon}\right\}.

It is easy to see that the stopping time
\nu_{n}
converges
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. to
\infty
as
n\to\infty
.
The following result gives the tail behavior of
\nu_{n}
.

Lemma 7.2
.

Assume
2
for some
\delta>0
.
Then, for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1/2)
and
\beta>0
,
there exists a constant
c_{\varepsilon,\beta}
such that for any
n\geqslant 1
and
x\geqslant 0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\nu_{n}>\beta n^{1-\varepsilon}\right)\leqslant c_{\varepsilon,\beta}e^{-c_{\varepsilon,\beta}n^{\varepsilon}}.

Proof.

Set
K:=[n^{\varepsilon}]
and
split the interval
[1,\beta n^{1-\varepsilon}]
into
K
subintervals of length
l:=[\beta n^{1-2\varepsilon}]
,
so that
Kl\leqslant\beta n^{1-\varepsilon}
.
We have for large enough
n
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\nu_{n}>\beta n^{1-\varepsilon}\big)\leqslant\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{1\leqslant j\leqslant Kl}|y+S_{j}|\leqslant n^{1/2-\varepsilon}\right).

For each
k=1,\ldots,K
,
denote
A_{k,x}:=\{\max_{1\leqslant k^{\prime}\leqslant k}|x+S_{k^{\prime}l}|\leqslant n^{1/2-\varepsilon}\}
.
Using the Markov property, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(A_{K,x})\leqslant\mathbb{P}(A_{K-1,x})\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{P}(A_{1,x})\leqslant\Big(\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{P}(A_{1,x})\Big)^{K}.

By the central limit theorem for
S_{l}
, we get
that uniformly in
x\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(A_{1,x})\leqslant\mathbb{P}\big(|x+S_{l}|\leqslant 2n^{1/2-\varepsilon}\big)\leqslant\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{-\frac{x}{\sqrt{l}}-c_{\varepsilon,\beta}}^{-\frac{x}{\sqrt{l}}+c_{\varepsilon,\beta}}e^{-\frac{u^{2}}{2}}du+cr_{l}<1,

where
c_{\varepsilon,\beta}=\frac{n^{1/2-\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{l}}
.
Since
K=[n^{\varepsilon}]
, the result follows. ∎

The following result shows that the expectation of
the random walk
(x+S_{n})_{n\geqslant 1}
killed at the exit time
\tau_{x}

is uniformly bounded with respect to
n
.

Lemma 7.3
.

Assume
2
for some
\delta>0
.
There exists
c>0
such that for any
x\geqslant 0
and
n\geqslant 1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(x+S_{n};\tau_{x}>n\right)\leqslant c(1+x).

Proof.

The proof is a combination of a recursive argument and the Markov property.
For brevity, denote
V_{n}(x)=\mathbb{E}(x+S_{n};\tau_{x}>n)
.
For
\varepsilon\in(0,\frac{\delta}{2(2+\delta)}]
, we have

\displaystyle V_{n}(x)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left(x+S_{n};\tau_{x}>n,\nu_{n}>[n^{1-\varepsilon}]\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\left(x+S_{n};\tau_{x}>n,\nu_{n}\leqslant[n^{1-\varepsilon}]\right)

(7.3)

\displaystyle=:J_{1}+J_{2}.

Bound of
J_{1}
.
By Cauchy-Schwarz’s inequality, Minkowski’s inequality and Lemma
7.2
,
we obtain that for any
\varepsilon\in(0,\frac{\delta}{2(2+\delta)}]
,

\displaystyle J_{1}

\displaystyle\leqslant\mathbb{E}\left(x+S_{n};\nu_{n}>[n^{1-\varepsilon}]\right)\leqslant\mathbb{E}^{1/2}\left(|x+S_{n}|^{2}\right)\mathbb{P}^{1/2}\left(\nu_{n}>[n^{1-\varepsilon}]\right)

(7.4)

\displaystyle\leqslant\left(x+cn^{1/2}\right)c_{\varepsilon}e^{-c_{\varepsilon}n^{\varepsilon}}\leqslant c_{\varepsilon}(1+x)e^{-c_{\varepsilon}n^{\varepsilon}}.

Bound of
J_{2}
.
Using the Markov property leads to

\displaystyle J_{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{[n^{1-\varepsilon}]}\mathbb{E}(x+S_{n};\tau_{x}>n,\nu_{n}=k)

(7.5)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{[n^{1-\varepsilon}]}\int_{\mathbb{R}}V_{n-k}(x^{\prime})\mathbb{P}\left(x+S_{k}\in dx^{\prime};\tau_{x}>k,\nu_{n}=k\right).

Note that on the event
\{\tau_{x}>k,\nu_{n}=k\}
, we have
x^{\prime}=x+S_{k}>n^{1/2-\varepsilon}
.
By Lemma
7.1
, it holds that for any
\varepsilon\in(0,\frac{\delta}{2(2+\delta)}]
,

\displaystyle V_{n-k}(x^{\prime})=\mathbb{E}\left(x^{\prime}+S_{n-k};\tau_{x^{\prime}}>n-k\right)\leqslant\left(1+\frac{c}{(n-k)^{\varepsilon}}\right)x^{\prime}.

Implementing this bound into
(
7.1
)
, we derive that

\displaystyle J_{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{k=1}^{[n^{1-\varepsilon}]}\left(1+\frac{c}{(n-k)^{\varepsilon}}\right)\mathbb{E}(x+S_{k};\tau_{x}>k,\nu_{n}=k)

(7.6)

\displaystyle\leqslant\left(1+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon}}\right)\sum_{k=1}^{[n^{1-\varepsilon}]}\mathbb{E}(x+S_{k};\tau_{x}>k,\nu_{n}=k).

Since
\big((x+S_{n})\mathds{1}_{\{\tau_{x}>n\}}\big)_{n\geqslant 1}
is a submartingale with respect to the natural filtration

\mathscr{F}_{n}=\sigma(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})
,
we get that for any
1\leqslant k\leqslant[n^{1-\varepsilon}]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(x+S_{k};\tau_{x}>k,\nu_{n}=k)\leqslant\mathbb{E}\left(x+S_{[n^{1-\varepsilon}]};\tau_{x}>[n^{1-\varepsilon}],\nu_{n}=k\right),

and consequently, for any
\varepsilon\in(0,\frac{\delta}{2(2+\delta)}]
,

(7.7)

\displaystyle J_{2}\leqslant\left(1+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon}}\right)V_{[n^{1-\varepsilon}]}(x).

Substituting (
7.7
) and
(
7.1
)
into
(
7.1
)
gives

\displaystyle V_{n}(x)\leqslant\left(1+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon}}\right)V_{[n^{1-\varepsilon}]}(x)+c_{\varepsilon}(1+x)e^{-c_{\varepsilon}n^{\varepsilon}}.

Applying
[
25
, Lemma A.1]
, we get that for any integer
k_{0}\in[1,n]
,

\displaystyle V_{n}(x)\leqslant\left(1+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{k_{0}^{\varepsilon}}\right)V_{k_{0}}(x)+c_{\varepsilon}(1+x)e^{-c_{\varepsilon}k_{0}^{\varepsilon}}.

By Lemma
7.1
, we have
V_{k_{0}}(x)\leqslant x+ck_{0}^{1/2-\varepsilon}

and therefore, for any
\varepsilon\in(0,\frac{\delta}{2(2+\delta)}]
,

\displaystyle V_{n}(x)

\displaystyle\leqslant\left(1+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{k_{0}^{\varepsilon}}\right)x+ck_{0}^{1/2-\varepsilon}+c_{\varepsilon}(1+x)e^{-c_{\varepsilon}k_{0}^{\varepsilon}}

(7.8)

\displaystyle\leqslant\left(1+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}}{k_{0}^{\varepsilon}}\right)x+c_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}k_{0}^{1/2-\varepsilon},

which concludes the proof of the lemma.
∎

The following result proves the existence and gives some properties of the harmonic function
V
.

Lemma 7.4
.

Assume
2
for some
\delta>0
.

1. For any
x\geqslant 0
, the limit
\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\left(x+S_{n};\tau_{x}>n\right)

exists and

(7.9)

\displaystyle V(x):=\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\left(x+S_{n};\tau_{x}>n\right).

2. The function
V
is increasing on
\mathbb{R}_{+}

and
x\leqslant V(x)\leqslant c(1+x)
for any
x>0
. Moreover,
\lim_{x\to\infty}\frac{V(x)}{x}=1.

3. The function
V
is harmonic in the sense that for any
x\geqslant 0,

(7.10)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(V\left(x+S_{1}\right);\tau_{x}>1\right)=V(x).

Proof.

We proceed with the first assertion. Using (
7.1
) and Lemma
7.3
, we get that for
x\geqslant 0
,

(7.11)

\displaystyle x\mathbb{P}(\tau_{x}>n)+\mathbb{E}(-S_{\tau_{x}};\tau_{x}\leqslant n)=\mathbb{E}(x+S_{n};\tau_{x}>n)\leqslant c(1+x).

Since
x+S_{\tau_{x}}\leqslant 0
, we have
S_{\tau_{x}}\leqslant 0
and hence, using (
7.11
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(-S_{\tau_{x}};\tau_{x}\leqslant n)\leqslant c(1+x).

By the Lebesgue monotone convergence theorem,
it follows that

(7.12)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}(-S_{\tau_{x}};\tau_{x}\leqslant n)=\mathbb{E}(-S_{\tau_{x}})\leqslant c(1+x).

This, together with (
7.11
) and the fact that
\mathbb{P}(\tau_{x}>n)\to 0
as
n\to\infty
, proves (
7.9
).

We now prove the second assertion. Since for any
0\leqslant x_{1}\leqslant x_{2}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(x_{1}+S_{n};\tau_{x_{1}}>n)\leqslant\mathbb{E}(x_{2}+S_{n};\tau_{x_{2}}>n),

from (
7.9
) it follows that
V
is increasing on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
.

Using (
7.1
) and the fact that
x+S_{\tau_{x}}\leqslant 0
, we see that

\mathbb{E}(x+S_{n};\tau_{x}>n)\geqslant x
.
Hence, by (
7.9
), we get
V(x)\geqslant x
.
Since
V(x)=-\mathbb{E}S_{\tau_{x}}
, using (
7.12
) we get that
V(x)\leqslant c(1+x)
for some constant
c>0
.
Therefore
x\leqslant V(x)\leqslant c(1+x)
.

From (
7.1
) and (
7.9
), it follows that

\displaystyle\limsup_{x\to\infty}\frac{V(x)}{x}\leqslant 1+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{k_{0}^{\varepsilon}}.

Taking
k_{0}\to\infty
, we get
\limsup_{x\to\infty}V(x)/x\leqslant 1
.
This, together with the inequality
x\leqslant V(x)
, shows that
\lim_{x\to\infty}V(x)/x=1
.

For the third assertion, denoting
V_{n}(x)=\mathbb{E}(x+S_{n};\tau_{x}>n)
, by the Markov property, we have

\displaystyle V_{n+1}(x)=\int_{\mathbb{R}}V_{n}(x^{\prime})\mathbb{P}\left(x+S_{1}\in dx^{\prime},\tau_{x}>1\right).

The identity (
7.10
) follows from the Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem,
the inequality
V_{n}(x^{\prime})\leqslant c(1+x^{\prime})
and (
7.9
).
∎

Proof of Lemma
3.7
.

The assertion of the lemma follows from (
7.1
) and (
7.9
).
∎

Recall that
\nu_{n}
is a stopping time defined by (
7.2
).

Lemma 7.5
.

Assume
2
for some
\delta>0
.
Then, for any
\varepsilon\in(0,\frac{\delta}{2(2+\delta)}]
, there exists a constant
c_{\varepsilon}>0
such that
for any
x\geqslant 0
and
n\geqslant 1
,

(7.13)

\displaystyle E_{1}:=\mathbb{E}\left(x+S_{\nu_{n}};\tau_{x}>\nu_{n},\nu_{n}\leqslant[n^{1-\varepsilon}]\right)\leqslant c_{\varepsilon}(1+x),

and

(7.14)

\displaystyle E_{1}\leqslant V(x)\leqslant\left(1+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon/2}}\right)E_{1}+c_{\varepsilon}(1+x)e^{-c_{\varepsilon}n^{\varepsilon}}.

Proof.

We first prove (
7.13
).
Since
x+S_{\nu_{n}}>0
, we have

\displaystyle E_{1}

\displaystyle\leqslant\mathbb{E}\left(x+S_{[n^{1-\varepsilon}]};\tau_{x}>[n^{1-\varepsilon}],\nu_{n}\leqslant[n^{1-\varepsilon}]\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\mathbb{E}\left(x+S_{[n^{1-\varepsilon}]};\tau_{x}>[n^{1-\varepsilon}]\right).

Using Lemma
7.3
, we obtain (
7.13
).

We next prove (
7.14
).
By Lemma
7.4

and the fact that
\big(V(x+S_{n})\mathds{1}_{\{\tau_{x}>n\}}\big)_{n\geqslant 0}
is a martingale
with respect to the filtration
\mathscr{F}_{n}=\sigma(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})
, we get

\displaystyle V(x)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left(V(x+S_{n});\tau_{x}>n,\nu_{n}\leqslant[n^{1-\varepsilon}]\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\left(V(x+S_{n});\tau_{x}>n,\nu_{n}>[n^{1-\varepsilon}]\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left(V(x+S_{\nu_{n}});\tau_{x}>\nu_{n},\nu_{n}\leqslant[n^{1-\varepsilon}]\right)

(7.15)

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\left(V(x+S_{n});\tau_{x}>n,\nu_{n}>[n^{1-\varepsilon}]\right).

On the one hand, since
V(x)\geqslant x
, from (
7.15
) we get

(7.16)

\displaystyle V(x)\geqslant\mathbb{E}\left(V(x+S_{\nu_{n}});\tau_{x}>\nu_{n},\nu_{n}\leqslant[n^{1-\varepsilon}]\right)\geqslant E_{1}.

On the other hand,
using Lemma
3.7
and the inequality
V(x)\leqslant c(1+x)
, we obtain
that for any
\varepsilon\in(0,\frac{\delta}{2(2+\delta)}]
,

\displaystyle V(x)

\displaystyle\leqslant\left(1+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{k_{0}^{\varepsilon}}\right)E_{1}+c_{\varepsilon}k_{0}^{1/2-\varepsilon}\mathbb{P}\left(\tau_{x}>\nu_{n},\nu_{n}\leqslant[n^{1-\varepsilon}]\right)

(7.17)

\displaystyle\quad+c\mathbb{E}\left(1+x+S_{n};\tau_{x}>n,\nu_{n}>[n^{1-\varepsilon}]\right).

By Hölder’s inequality and Lemma
7.2
, we get that for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1/2)
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(x+S_{n};\tau_{x}>n,\nu_{n}>[n^{1-\varepsilon}]\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant c(x+n^{1/2})\mathbb{P}^{1/2}\left(\nu_{n}>[n^{1-\varepsilon}]\right)

(7.18)

\displaystyle\leqslant c_{\varepsilon}(1+x)e^{-c_{\varepsilon}n^{\varepsilon}}.

Since
n^{1/2-\varepsilon}\leqslant x+S_{\nu_{n}}
on the event
\{\tau_{x}>\nu_{n}\}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\tau_{x}>\nu_{n},\nu_{n}\leqslant[n^{1-\varepsilon}]\right)

(7.19)

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{n^{1/2-\varepsilon}}\mathbb{E}\left(x+S_{\nu_{n}};\tau_{x}>\nu_{n},\nu_{n}\leqslant[n^{1-\varepsilon}]\right)=\frac{1}{n^{1/2-\varepsilon}}E_{1}.

Combining
(
7.1
)
,
(
7.1
)
and (
7.1
)
gives that for any
\varepsilon\in(0,\frac{\delta}{2(2+\delta)}]
,

\displaystyle V(x)\leqslant\left(1+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{k_{0}^{\varepsilon}}+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}k_{0}^{1/2-\varepsilon}}{n^{1/2-\varepsilon}}\right)E_{1}+c_{\varepsilon}(1+x)e^{-c_{\varepsilon}n^{\varepsilon}}.

By taking
k_{0}=n^{1-2\varepsilon}
, this implies that

(7.20)

\displaystyle V(x)\leqslant\left(1+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon(1-\varepsilon)}}\right)E_{1}+c_{\varepsilon}(1+x)e^{-c_{\varepsilon}n^{\varepsilon}}.

Combining this with (
7.16
),
we get (
7.14
). ∎

Lemma 7.6
.

Assume
2
for some
\delta>0
.
Then, for any
\varepsilon\in(0,\frac{\delta}{2(2+\delta)}]
, there exists a constant
c_{\varepsilon}>0
such that
for any
x\geqslant 0
and
n\geqslant 1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\tau_{x}>n)\leqslant\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{1/2-\varepsilon}}(1+x).

Proof.

By Lemmas
7.5
and
7.2
, we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\tau_{x}>n)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\tau_{x}>n,\nu_{n}\leqslant[n^{1-\varepsilon}]\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\tau_{x}>n,\nu_{n}>[n^{1-\varepsilon}]\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{n^{1/2-\varepsilon}}\mathbb{E}\left(x+S_{\nu_{n}};\tau_{x}>\nu_{n},\nu_{n}\leqslant[n^{1-\varepsilon}]\right)+c_{\varepsilon}e^{-c_{\varepsilon}n^{\varepsilon}}.

Using the bound (
7.13
), the result follows.
∎

Lemma 7.7
.

Assume
2
for some
\delta>0
.
There exists
\varepsilon_{0}>0
such that for any
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})
,

x\geqslant 0
and
n\geqslant 1
,

\displaystyle E_{2}:=\mathbb{E}\left(x+S_{\nu_{n}};x+S_{\nu_{n}}>n^{1/2-\varepsilon/2},\tau_{x}>\nu_{n},\nu_{n}\leqslant[n^{1-\varepsilon}]\right)\leqslant\frac{c_{\varepsilon}(1+x)}{n^{\delta/2-\varepsilon(1+\varepsilon+\delta/2)}}.

Proof.

Since
x+S_{\nu_{n}}\leqslant X_{\nu_{n}}+n^{1/2-\varepsilon}

on the event
\{x+S_{\nu_{n}}>n^{1/2-\varepsilon/2}\}
,
we have

\displaystyle E_{2}\leqslant\mathbb{E}\left(x+S_{\nu_{n}};X_{\nu_{n}}>n^{1/2-\varepsilon/2}-n^{1/2-\varepsilon},\tau_{x}>\nu_{n},\nu_{n}\leqslant[n^{1-\varepsilon}]\right).

Since

n^{1/2-\varepsilon/2}-n^{1/2-\varepsilon}>c_{\varepsilon}n^{1/2-\varepsilon/2}
,
it follows that

\displaystyle E_{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{k=1}^{[n^{1-\varepsilon}]}\mathbb{E}\left(x+S_{k};X_{k}>c_{\varepsilon}n^{1/2-\varepsilon/2},\tau_{x}>k\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{k=1}^{[n^{1-\varepsilon}]}\mathbb{E}\left(x+S_{k-1}+X_{k};X_{k}>c_{\varepsilon}n^{1/2-\varepsilon/2},\tau_{x}>k-1\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{[n^{1-\varepsilon}]}\mathbb{E}\left(x+S_{k-1};\tau_{x}>k-1\right)\mathbb{P}\left(X_{k}>c_{\varepsilon}n^{1/2-\varepsilon/2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{k=1}^{[n^{1-\varepsilon}]}\mathbb{E}\left(X_{k};X_{k}>c_{\varepsilon}n^{1/2-\varepsilon/2}\right)\mathbb{P}\left(\tau_{x}>k-1\right)

\displaystyle=:E_{21}+E_{22}.

For
E_{21}
, using Lemma
7.3
, Markov’s inequality and condition
2
,
we get

\displaystyle E_{21}\leqslant c(1+x)\sum_{k=1}^{[n^{1-\varepsilon}]}\mathbb{P}\left(X_{k}>c_{\varepsilon}n^{1/2-\varepsilon/2}\right)\leqslant\frac{c(1+x)}{n^{\delta/2-\varepsilon\delta/2}}.

For
E_{22}
, using Lemma
7.6
and again Markov’s inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle E_{22}

\displaystyle\leqslant\mathbb{E}\left(X_{1};X_{1}>c_{\varepsilon}n^{1/2-\varepsilon/2}\right)\sum_{k=1}^{[n^{1-\varepsilon}]}\mathbb{P}\left(\tau_{x}>k-1\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{(1/2-\varepsilon/2)(1+\delta)}}\sum_{k=1}^{[n^{1-\varepsilon}]}\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{k^{1/2-\varepsilon}}(1+x)

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{(1/2-\varepsilon/2)(1+\delta)}}c_{\varepsilon}(1+x)n^{(1-\varepsilon)(1/2+\varepsilon)}\leqslant\frac{c_{\varepsilon}(1+x)}{n^{\delta/2-\varepsilon(1+\varepsilon+\delta/2)}}.

The desired result follows.
∎

7.2.
Proof of Theorems
2.7
and
2.8

We first give a proof of Theorem
2.7

by using the bounds shown in Section
7.1

and the functional central limit theorem (Lemma
3.6
).

Proof of Theorem
2.7
.

As in (
3.40
), denote

(7.21)

\displaystyle A_{k}=\left\{\sup_{0\leqslant t\leqslant 1}\left|S_{[tk]}-B_{tk}\right|\leqslant k^{1/2-2\varepsilon}\right\}

and by
A_{k}^{c}
its complement.
Using the Markov property, we have

(7.22)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(x+S_{n}\leqslant t\sqrt{n},\tau_{x}>n\right)=:J_{1}+J_{2}+J_{3},

where

\displaystyle J_{1}=\mathbb{P}\left(x+S_{n}\leqslant t\sqrt{n},\tau_{x}>n,\nu_{n}>[n^{1-\varepsilon}]\right),

\displaystyle J_{2}=\sum_{k=1}^{[n^{1-\varepsilon}]}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{P}(x^{\prime}+S_{n-k}\leqslant t\sqrt{n},\tau_{x^{\prime}}>n-k,A_{n-k}^{c})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\mathbb{P}\left(x+S_{k}\in dx^{\prime},\tau_{x}>k,\nu_{n}=k\right),

\displaystyle J_{3}=\sum_{k=1}^{[n^{1-\varepsilon}]}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{P}(x^{\prime}+S_{n-k}\leqslant t\sqrt{n},\tau_{x^{\prime}}>n-k,A_{n-k})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\mathbb{P}\left(x+S_{k}\in dx^{\prime},\tau_{x}>k,\nu_{n}=k\right).

Bound of
J_{1}
.
By Lemma
7.2
, it holds that

(7.23)

\displaystyle J_{1}\leqslant\mathbb{P}\left(\nu_{n}>[n^{1-\varepsilon}]\right)\leqslant c_{\varepsilon}e^{-c_{\varepsilon}n^{\varepsilon}}.

Bound of
J_{2}
.
For
k\leqslant[n^{1-\varepsilon}]
, we have
n-k\geqslant c_{\varepsilon}n
.
By Lemma
3.6
,

(7.24)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(x^{\prime}+S_{n-k}\leqslant t\sqrt{n},\tau_{x^{\prime}}>n-k,A_{n-k}^{c}\right)\leqslant\mathbb{P}\left(A_{n-k}^{c}\right)\leqslant\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\delta/2-2\varepsilon(2+\delta)}}.

Note that on the event
\{\tau_{x}>\nu_{n}\}
,
we have
n^{1/2-\varepsilon}\leqslant x+S_{\nu_{n}}
.
By Lemma
7.5
, we get

\displaystyle J_{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\delta/2-2\varepsilon(2+\delta)}}\mathbb{P}\left(\tau_{x}>\nu_{n},\nu_{n}\leqslant[n^{1-\varepsilon}]\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{(\delta+1)/2-2\varepsilon(2+\delta)-\varepsilon}}\mathbb{E}\left(x+S_{\nu_{n}};\tau_{x}>\nu_{n},\nu_{n}\leqslant[n^{1-\varepsilon}]\right)

(7.25)

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{c_{\varepsilon}(1+x)}{n^{(\delta+1)/2-\varepsilon(5+2\delta)}}.

Bound of
J_{3}
.
We write
J_{3}=J_{31}+J_{32}
, where

\displaystyle J_{31}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{[n^{1-\varepsilon}]}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{P}(x^{\prime}+S_{n-k}\leqslant t\sqrt{n},\tau_{x^{\prime}}>n-k,A_{n-k})

\displaystyle\qquad\times\mathbb{P}(x+S_{k}\in dx^{\prime},x+S_{k}>n^{1/2-\varepsilon/2},\tau_{x}>k,\nu_{n}=k),

\displaystyle J_{32}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{[n^{1-\varepsilon}]}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{P}(x^{\prime}+S_{n-k}\leqslant t\sqrt{n},\tau_{x^{\prime}}>n-k,A_{n-k})

\displaystyle\qquad\times\mathbb{P}(x+S_{k}\in dx^{\prime},x+S_{k}\leqslant n^{1/2-\varepsilon/2},\tau_{x}>k,\nu_{n}=k).

Bound of
J_{31}
.
Denote
x_{+}^{\prime}=x^{\prime}+(n-k)^{1/2-2\varepsilon}
.
Since
x^{\prime}>n^{1/2-\varepsilon/2}
, from (
7.21
) and (
3.8
), we derive that

(7.26)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\tau_{x^{\prime}}>n-k,A_{n-k}\right)\leqslant\mathbb{P}\left(\tau_{x_{+}^{\prime}}^{bm}>n-k\right)\leqslant c\frac{x_{+}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{n-k}}\leqslant\frac{c_{\varepsilon}x^{\prime}}{\sqrt{n}}.

Implementing the bound (
7.26
) into
J_{31}
,
and using Lemma
7.7
, we obtain

(7.27)

\displaystyle J_{31}\leqslant\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{n}}E_{2}\leqslant\frac{c_{\varepsilon}(1+x)}{n^{(1+\delta)/2-\varepsilon(1+\varepsilon+\delta/2)}}.

Upper bound of
J_{32}
.
Denote
x_{+}^{\prime}=x^{\prime}+(n-k)^{1/2-2\varepsilon}

and
t_{+}=t+\frac{1}{n^{2\varepsilon}}
.
By Lemma
3.5
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}(x^{\prime}):

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}(x^{\prime}+S_{n-k}\leqslant t\sqrt{n},\tau_{x^{\prime}}>n-k,A_{n-k})

\displaystyle\leqslant\mathbb{P}\left(x_{+}^{\prime}+\sigma B_{n-k}\leqslant t_{+}\sqrt{n},\tau_{x_{+}^{\prime}}^{bm}>n-k\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma\sqrt{2\pi(n-k)}}\int_{0}^{\sqrt{n}t_{+}}\Big(e^{-\frac{(u-x_{+}^{\prime})^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}(n-k)}}-e^{-\frac{(u+x_{+}^{\prime})^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}(n-k)}}\Big)du

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{\sigma\sqrt{2\pi(n-k)}}\int_{0}^{\sqrt{n}t_{+}}e^{-\frac{u^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}(n-k)}}\Big(e^{\frac{ux_{+}^{\prime}}{\sigma^{2}(n-k)}}-e^{-\frac{ux_{+}^{\prime}}{\sigma^{2}(n-k)}}\Big)du

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{0}^{\sqrt{\frac{n}{n-k}}t_{+}}e^{-\frac{s^{2}}{2}}\Big(e^{s\frac{x_{+}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{n-k}}}-e^{-s\frac{x_{+}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{n-k}}}\Big)ds

(7.28)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{0}^{\sqrt{\frac{n}{n-k}}t_{+}}e^{-\frac{s^{2}}{2}}e^{s\frac{x_{+}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{n-k}}}\Big(1-e^{-2s\frac{x_{+}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{n-k}}}\Big)ds.

Using the inequality
1-e^{-z}\leqslant z
for
z\geqslant 0
, it follows that, with
t_{+}=t+\frac{1}{n^{2\varepsilon}}
,

(7.29)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}(x^{\prime})\leqslant\frac{2x_{+}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{2\pi(n-k)}}\int_{0}^{\sqrt{\frac{n}{n-k}}t_{+}}se^{-\frac{s^{2}}{2}}e^{s\frac{x_{+}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{n-k}}}ds.

Since
k\leqslant[n^{1-\varepsilon}]
and
x_{+}^{\prime}=x^{\prime}+(n-k)^{1/2-2\varepsilon}
with
x^{\prime}\leqslant n^{1/2-\varepsilon/2}
,
we have

\displaystyle\frac{x_{+}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{n-k}}\leqslant\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon/2}}.

Hence, when
t\in[0,n^{\varepsilon^{p}}]
for some constant
p\geqslant 2
sufficiently large,
we have
s\leqslant\sqrt{\frac{n}{n-k}}t_{+}\leqslant c_{\varepsilon}n^{\varepsilon^{p}}
,
so that

\displaystyle\exp\left(s\frac{x_{+}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{n-k}}\right)\leqslant\exp\left(\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon/2-\varepsilon^{p}}}\right)\leqslant 1+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon/2-\varepsilon^{p}}}.

This implies that when
t\in[0,n^{\varepsilon^{p}}]
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\sqrt{\frac{n}{n-k}}t_{+}}se^{-\frac{s^{2}}{2}}e^{s\frac{x_{+}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{n-k}}}ds

\displaystyle\leqslant\left(1+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon/2-\varepsilon^{p}}}\right)\int_{0}^{\sqrt{\frac{n}{n-k}}t_{+}}se^{-\frac{s^{2}}{2}}ds

\displaystyle\leqslant\left(1+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon/2-\varepsilon^{p}}}\right)\left(\Phi^{+}(t)+\sqrt{\frac{n}{n-k}}t_{+}-t\right)

(7.30)

\displaystyle\leqslant\left(1+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon/2-\varepsilon^{p}}}\right)\left(\Phi^{+}(t)+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon}}\right).

When
t>n^{\varepsilon^{p}}
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\sqrt{\frac{n}{n-k}}t_{+}}se^{-\frac{s^{2}}{2}}e^{s\frac{x_{+}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{n-k}}}ds

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{n^{\varepsilon^{p}}}se^{-\frac{s^{2}}{2}}e^{s\frac{x_{+}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{n-k}}}ds+\int_{n^{\varepsilon^{p}}}^{\sqrt{\frac{n}{n-k}}t_{+}}se^{-\frac{s^{2}}{2}}e^{s\frac{x_{+}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{n-k}}}ds

\displaystyle\leqslant\left(1+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon/2-\varepsilon^{p}}}\right)\int_{0}^{n^{\varepsilon^{p}}}se^{-\frac{s^{2}}{2}}ds+c_{\varepsilon}e^{-c_{\varepsilon}n^{\varepsilon^{p}}}

(7.31)

\displaystyle\leqslant\left(1+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon/2-\varepsilon^{p}}}\right)\int_{0}^{t}se^{-\frac{s^{2}}{2}}ds+c_{\varepsilon}e^{-c_{\varepsilon}n^{\varepsilon^{p}}}.

Combining (
7.29
), (
7.2
) and (
7.2
), we get that uniformly in
t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}(x^{\prime})\leqslant\frac{2x_{+}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{2\pi(n-k)}}\left(1+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon/2-\varepsilon^{p}}}\right)\left(\Phi^{+}(t)+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon}}\right).

Since
k\leqslant[n^{1-\varepsilon}]
,
we have
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n-k}}\leqslant\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}(1+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon}})

and

\displaystyle\frac{x_{+}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{n-k}}\leqslant\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left(x^{\prime}+n^{1/2-2\varepsilon}\right)\left(1+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon}}\right),

which implies that

(7.32)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}(x^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{2}{\sqrt{2\pi n}}\left(x^{\prime}+n^{1/2-2\varepsilon}\right)\left(1+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon/2-\varepsilon^{p}}}\right)\left(\Phi^{+}(t)+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon}}\right).

Therefore, using the fact that
n^{1/2-\varepsilon}\leqslant x+S_{\nu_{n}}
, we get

\displaystyle J_{32}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{2}{\sqrt{2\pi n}}\left(1+\frac{c_{\varepsilon,p}}{n^{\varepsilon/2-\varepsilon^{p}}}\right)\left(\Phi^{+}(t)+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\times\sum_{k=1}^{[n^{1-\varepsilon}]}\mathbb{E}\left(x+S_{k}+n^{1/2-2\varepsilon},x+S_{k}\leqslant n^{1/2-\varepsilon/2},\tau_{x}>k,\nu_{n}=k\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{2}{\sqrt{2\pi n}}\left(1+\frac{c_{\varepsilon,p}}{n^{\varepsilon/2-\varepsilon^{p}}}\right)\left(\Phi^{+}(t)+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\times\mathbb{E}\left(x+S_{\nu_{n}},x+S_{\nu_{n}}\leqslant n^{1/2-\varepsilon/2},\tau_{x}>\nu_{n},\nu_{n}\leqslant[n^{1-\varepsilon}]\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{2}{\sqrt{2\pi n}}\left(1+\frac{c_{\varepsilon,p}}{n^{\varepsilon/2-\varepsilon^{p}}}\right)\left(\Phi^{+}(t)+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon}}\right)E_{1}

(7.33)

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{2V(x)}{\sqrt{2\pi n}}\left(1+\frac{c_{\varepsilon,p}}{n^{\varepsilon/2-\varepsilon^{p}}}\right)\left(\Phi^{+}(t)+\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon}}\right),

where in the last inequality we used Lemma
7.5
.

Lower bound of
J_{32}
.
Denote
x_{-}^{\prime}=x^{\prime}-(n-k)^{1/2-2\varepsilon}
and
t_{-}=t-n^{-2\varepsilon}
.
By Lemma
3.5
, we have that for
t\geqslant n^{-2\varepsilon}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}(x^{\prime})

\displaystyle\geqslant\mathbb{P}\left(x_{-}^{\prime}+B_{n-k}\leqslant t_{-}\sqrt{n},\tau_{x_{-}^{\prime}}^{bm}>n-k\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi(n-k)}}\int_{0}^{\sqrt{n}t_{-}}\Big(e^{-\frac{(u-x_{-}^{\prime})^{2}}{2(n-k)}}-e^{-\frac{(u+x_{-}^{\prime})^{2}}{2(n-k)}}\Big)du

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi(n-k)}}e^{-\frac{(x_{-}^{\prime})^{2}}{2(n-k)}}\int_{0}^{\sqrt{n}t_{-}}e^{-\frac{u^{2}}{2(n-k)}}\Big(e^{\frac{ux_{-}^{\prime}}{n-k}}-e^{-\frac{ux_{-}^{\prime}}{n-k}}\Big)du

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}e^{-\frac{(x_{-}^{\prime})^{2}}{2(n-k)}}\int_{0}^{\sqrt{\frac{n}{n-k}}t_{-}}e^{-\frac{s^{2}}{2}}\Big(e^{s\frac{x_{-}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{n-k}}}-e^{-s\frac{x_{-}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{n-k}}}\Big)ds

(7.34)

\displaystyle\geqslant\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}e^{-\frac{(x_{-}^{\prime})^{2}}{2(n-k)}}\int_{0}^{t_{-}}e^{-\frac{s^{2}}{2}}e^{-s\frac{x_{-}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{n-k}}}\Big(e^{2s\frac{x_{-}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{n-k}}}-1\Big)ds.

Using the inequality
e^{z}-1\geqslant z
for
z\in\mathbb{R}
, we get

(7.35)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}(x^{\prime})\geqslant\frac{2x_{-}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{2\pi(n-k)}}e^{-\frac{(x_{-}^{\prime})^{2}}{2(n-k)}}\int_{0}^{t_{-}}se^{-\frac{s^{2}}{2}}e^{-s\frac{x_{-}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{n-k}}}ds.

Since
x^{\prime}\leqslant n^{1/2-\varepsilon/2}
,
x_{-}^{\prime}=x^{\prime}-(n-k)^{1/2-2\varepsilon}
and
k\leqslant[n^{1-\varepsilon}]
, we have

(7.36)

\displaystyle\left|\frac{x_{-}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{n-k}}\right|\leqslant\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon/2}},\qquad e^{-\frac{(x_{-}^{\prime})^{2}}{2(n-k)}}\geqslant\left(1-\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon}}\right).

Hence, when
t_{-}\in[0,n^{\varepsilon^{p}}]
for some constant
p\geqslant 2
sufficiently large,
we have
s\leqslant n^{\varepsilon^{p}}
,
so that

\displaystyle\exp\left(-s\frac{x_{-}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{n-k}}\right)\geqslant\exp\left(-\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon/2-\varepsilon^{p}}}\right)\geqslant 1-\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon/2-\varepsilon^{p}}}.

This implies that when
t_{-}\in[0,n^{\varepsilon^{p}}]
,

(7.37)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t_{-}}se^{-\frac{s^{2}}{2}}e^{-s\frac{x_{-}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{n-k}}}ds\geqslant\left(1-\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon/2-\varepsilon^{p}}}\right)\int_{0}^{t_{-}}se^{-\frac{s^{2}}{2}}ds.

When
t_{-}>n^{\varepsilon^{p}}
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t_{-}}se^{-\frac{s^{2}}{2}}e^{-s\frac{x_{-}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{n-k}}}ds

\displaystyle\geqslant\int_{0}^{n^{\varepsilon^{p}}}se^{-\frac{s^{2}}{2}}e^{-s\frac{x_{-}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{n-k}}}ds

\displaystyle\geqslant\left(1-\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon/2-\varepsilon^{p}}}\right)\int_{0}^{n^{\varepsilon^{p}}}se^{-\frac{s^{2}}{2}}ds

(7.38)

\displaystyle\geqslant\left(1-\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon/2-\varepsilon^{p}}}\right)\int_{0}^{t_{-}}se^{-\frac{s^{2}}{2}}ds-c_{\varepsilon}e^{-c_{\varepsilon}n^{\varepsilon^{p}}}.

Combining (
7.35
), (
7.36
),
(
7.37
) and (
7.2
),
we get that uniformly in
t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}(x^{\prime})\geqslant\frac{2x_{-}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{2\pi(n-k)}}\left[\left(1-\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon/2-\varepsilon^{p}}}\right)\int_{0}^{t_{-}}se^{-\frac{s^{2}}{2}}ds-c_{\varepsilon}e^{-c_{\varepsilon}n^{\varepsilon^{p}}}\right].

Since
k\leqslant[n^{1-\varepsilon}]
and
x_{-}^{\prime}=x^{\prime}-(n-k)^{1/2-2\varepsilon}
,
we have

\displaystyle\frac{x_{-}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{n-k}}\geqslant\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left(x^{\prime}-c_{\varepsilon}n^{1/2-2\varepsilon}\right).

It follows that

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}(x^{\prime})

\displaystyle\geqslant\frac{2}{\sqrt{2\pi n}}\left(x^{\prime}-c_{\varepsilon}n^{1/2-2\varepsilon}\right)\left(1-\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon/2-\varepsilon^{p}}}\right)\left(\int_{0}^{t_{-}}se^{-\frac{s^{2}}{2}}ds-c_{\varepsilon}e^{-c_{\varepsilon}n^{\varepsilon^{p}}}\right)

(7.39)

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{\sqrt{2\pi n}}\left(x^{\prime}-c_{\varepsilon}n^{1/2-2\varepsilon}\right)\left(1-\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon/2-\varepsilon^{p}}}\right)\left[\Phi^{+}(t_{-})-c_{\varepsilon}e^{-c_{\varepsilon}n^{\varepsilon^{p}}}\right].

Following the proof of (
7.2
),
using Lemmas
7.5
and
7.7
, we obtain

\displaystyle J_{32}

\displaystyle\geqslant\frac{2}{\sqrt{2\pi n}}\left(1-\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon/2-\varepsilon^{p}}}\right)\left[\Phi^{+}(t_{-})-c_{\varepsilon}e^{-c_{\varepsilon}n^{\varepsilon^{p}}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\times\sum_{k=1}^{[n^{1-\varepsilon}]}\mathbb{E}\left(x+S_{k}-c_{\varepsilon}n^{1/2-2\varepsilon},x+S_{k}\leqslant n^{1/2-\varepsilon/2},\tau_{x}>k,\nu_{n}=k\right)

\displaystyle\geqslant\frac{2}{\sqrt{2\pi n}}\left(1-\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon/2-\varepsilon^{p}}}\right)\left[\Phi^{+}(t_{-})-c_{\varepsilon}e^{-c_{\varepsilon}n^{\varepsilon^{p}}}\right]\left(E_{1}-E_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\geqslant\frac{2}{\sqrt{2\pi n}}\left(1-\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon/2-\varepsilon^{p}}}\right)\left[\Phi^{+}(t_{-})-c_{\varepsilon}e^{-c_{\varepsilon}n^{\varepsilon^{p}}}\right]\left(V(x)-\frac{c_{\varepsilon}(1+x)}{n^{\delta/2-\varepsilon(1+\varepsilon+\delta/2)}}\right)

(7.40)

\displaystyle\geqslant\frac{2V(x)}{\sqrt{2\pi n}}\left(1-\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon/2-\varepsilon^{p}}}\right)\Phi^{+}(t_{-})-\frac{c_{\varepsilon}(1+x)}{n^{\delta/2-\varepsilon(1+\varepsilon+\delta/2)}}.

We conclude the proof of Theorem
2.7
by combining (
7.23
), (
7.2
),
(
7.27
), (
7.2
) and (
7.2
).
∎

To prove Theorem
2.8
, we first show the following result which is based on the coupling method
using the functional central limit theorem (Lemma
3.6
).

Theorem 7.8
.

Assume
2
with some
\delta>0
.
For any
\gamma\in(0,\frac{\delta}{2(2+\delta)})
,
there exists a constant
c_{\gamma}>0
such that the following assertions hold:

1. For any
n\geqslant 1
,
x>0
and
t>0
,

(7.41)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{x+S_{n}}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}>t,\tau_{x}>n\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\int_{t}^{\infty}\psi\left(s,\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}+\frac{1}{\sigma n^{\gamma}}\right)ds+\frac{c_{\gamma}}{n^{\frac{\delta}{2}-\gamma(2+\delta)}}.

2. For any
n\geqslant 1
,
x>n^{1/2-\gamma}
and
t>0
,

(7.42)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{x+S_{n}}{\sqrt{n}}>t,\tau_{x}>n\right)

\displaystyle\geqslant\int_{t}^{\infty}\psi\left(s,\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}-\frac{1}{\sigma n^{\gamma}}\right)ds-\frac{c_{\gamma}}{n^{\frac{\delta}{2}-\gamma(2+\delta)}}.

Proof.

Denote

(7.43)

\displaystyle A_{n}=\left\{\sup_{0\leqslant t\leqslant 1}\left|S_{[tn]}-\sigma B_{tn}\right|>n^{1/2-\gamma}\right\}

and by
A_{n}^{c}
the complement of
A_{n}
.
By Lemma
3.6
,
for any
\gamma\in(0,\frac{\delta}{2(2+\delta)})
,
there exists a constant
c_{\gamma}>0
such that

(7.44)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(A_{n}\right)\leqslant\frac{c_{\gamma}}{n^{\frac{\delta}{2}-\gamma(2+\delta)}}.

Since
x_{\gamma}^{+}=x+n^{\frac{1}{2}-\gamma}
,
using Lemma
3.5
and (
7.44
), we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{x+S_{n}}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}>t,\tau_{x}>n\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{x_{\gamma}^{+}+\sigma B_{n}}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}>t,\tau_{x_{\gamma}^{+}}^{bm}>n\right)+\mathbb{P}(A_{n})

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\int_{t\sigma\sqrt{n}}^{\infty}\psi\left(\frac{s}{\sigma\sqrt{n}},\frac{x_{\gamma}^{+}}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)ds+\frac{c_{\gamma}}{n^{\frac{\delta}{2}-\gamma(2+\delta)}},

which ends the proof of the upper bound (
7.41
).

Since
x_{\gamma}^{-}=x-n^{\frac{1}{2}-\gamma}
,
using Lemma
3.5
and (
7.44
),
we get that for any
x>n^{\frac{1}{2}-\gamma}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{x+S_{n}}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}>t,\tau_{x}>n\right)

\displaystyle\geqslant\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{x_{\gamma}^{-}+\sigma B_{n}}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}>t,\tau_{x_{\gamma}^{-}}^{bm}>n,A_{n}^{c}\right)

\displaystyle\geqslant\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{x_{\gamma}^{-}+\sigma B_{n}}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}>t,\tau_{x_{\gamma}^{-}}^{bm}>n\right)-\mathbb{P}(A_{n})

\displaystyle\geqslant\frac{1}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\int_{t\sigma\sqrt{n}}^{\infty}\psi\left(\frac{s}{\sigma\sqrt{n}},\frac{x_{\gamma}^{-}}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)ds-\frac{c_{\gamma}}{n^{\frac{\delta}{2}-\gamma(2+\delta)}},

which concludes the proof of the lower bound (
7.42
).
∎

Using Theorem
7.8
, we now prove Theorem
2.8
.

Proof of Theorem
2.8
.

By the definition of
\psi
(cf. (
1.4
)), we have for
t>0
,

\displaystyle\int_{t}^{\infty}\psi\left(s,\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}+\frac{1}{\sigma n^{\gamma}}\right)ds-\int_{t}^{\infty}\psi\left(s,\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)ds

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{t}^{\infty}\left(e^{-\frac{1}{2}(s-\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}-\frac{1}{\sigma n^{\gamma}})^{2}}-e^{-\frac{1}{2}(s-\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}})^{2}}\right)ds

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{t}^{\infty}\left(e^{-\frac{1}{2}(s+\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}})^{2}}-e^{-\frac{1}{2}(s+\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}+\frac{1}{\sigma n^{\gamma}})^{2}}\right)ds

\displaystyle=:I_{1}+I_{2}.

For
I_{1}
, by a change of variable and elementary calculations, we get

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{t-\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}}^{\infty}\left(e^{-\frac{1}{2}(s-\frac{1}{\sigma n^{\gamma}})^{2}}-e^{-\frac{1}{2}s^{2}}\right)ds

\displaystyle\leqslant\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|e^{-\frac{1}{2}(s-\frac{1}{\sigma n^{\gamma}})^{2}}-e^{-\frac{1}{2}s^{2}}\right|ds

\displaystyle=\int_{|s|\leqslant n^{\gamma/2}}\left|e^{-\frac{1}{2}(s-\frac{1}{\sigma n^{\gamma}})^{2}}-e^{-\frac{1}{2}s^{2}}\right|ds+\int_{|s|>n^{\gamma/2}}\left|e^{-\frac{1}{2}(s-\frac{1}{\sigma n^{\gamma}})^{2}}-e^{-\frac{1}{2}s^{2}}\right|ds

\displaystyle=:I_{11}+I_{12}.

For
I_{11}
, using the inequality
e^{x}-1\leqslant|x|+|x|e^{x}
,
x\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

\displaystyle I_{11}

\displaystyle=\int_{|s|\leqslant n^{\gamma/2}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}s^{2}}\left|e^{\frac{s}{\sigma n^{\gamma}}-\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}n^{2\gamma}}}-1\right|ds

\displaystyle\leqslant\int_{|s|\leqslant n^{\gamma/2}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}s^{2}}\left|\frac{s}{\sigma n^{\gamma}}-\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}n^{2\gamma}}\right|ds

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\int_{|s|\leqslant n^{\gamma/2}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}(s-\frac{1}{\sigma n^{\gamma}})^{2}}\left|\frac{s}{\sigma n^{\gamma}}-\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}n^{2\gamma}}\right|ds

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\int_{|s|\leqslant 2n^{\gamma/2}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}s^{2}}\left|\frac{s}{\sigma n^{\gamma}}-\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}n^{2\gamma}}\right|ds

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{2}{\sigma n^{\gamma}}\int_{|s|\leqslant 2n^{\gamma/2}}|s|e^{-\frac{1}{2}s^{2}}ds+\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}n^{2\gamma}}\int_{|s|\leqslant 2n^{\gamma/2}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}s^{2}}ds\leqslant\frac{c}{n^{\gamma}}.

For
I_{12}
, we have

\displaystyle I_{12}\leqslant\int_{n^{\gamma/2}-\frac{1}{n^{\gamma}}}^{n^{\gamma/2}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}s^{2}}ds+\int_{-n^{\gamma/2}-\frac{1}{n^{\gamma}}}^{-n^{\gamma/2}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}s^{2}}ds\leqslant\frac{2}{n^{\gamma}}.

Hence, we get
I_{1}\leqslant\frac{c}{n^{\gamma}}.
Similarly, one can also check that
I_{2}\leqslant\frac{c}{n^{\gamma}}.

Therefore,

(7.45)

\displaystyle\int_{t}^{\infty}\psi\left(s,\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}+\frac{1}{\sigma n^{\gamma}}\right)ds-\int_{t}^{\infty}\psi\left(s,\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)ds\leqslant\frac{c}{n^{\gamma}}.

Using (
7.41
) and taking
\gamma=\frac{\delta}{2(3+\delta)}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{x+S_{n}}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}>t,\tau_{x}>n\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\int_{t}^{\infty}\psi\left(s,\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)ds+\frac{c}{n^{\gamma}}+\frac{c_{\gamma}}{n^{\frac{\delta}{2}-\gamma(2+\delta)}}

(7.46)

\displaystyle\leqslant\int_{t}^{\infty}\psi\left(s,\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)ds+\frac{c_{\gamma}}{n^{\frac{\delta}{2(3+\delta)}}}.

To show the lower bound, we first write for
t>0
,

\displaystyle\int_{t}^{\infty}\psi\left(s,\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}-\frac{1}{\sigma n^{\gamma}}\right)ds-\int_{t}^{\infty}\psi\left(s,\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)ds

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{t}^{\infty}\left(e^{-\frac{1}{2}(s-\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}+\frac{1}{\sigma n^{\gamma}})^{2}}-e^{-\frac{1}{2}(s-\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}})^{2}}\right)ds

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{t}^{\infty}\left(e^{-\frac{1}{2}(s+\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}})^{2}}-e^{-\frac{1}{2}(s+\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}-\frac{1}{\sigma n^{\gamma}})^{2}}\right)ds

\displaystyle=:J_{1}+J_{2}.

For
J_{1}
, by a change of variable and elementary calculations, we get

\displaystyle J_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{t-\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}}^{\infty}\left(e^{-\frac{1}{2}(s+\frac{1}{\sigma n^{\gamma}})^{2}}-e^{-\frac{1}{2}s^{2}}\right)ds

\displaystyle\geqslant-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|e^{-\frac{1}{2}(s+\frac{1}{\sigma n^{\gamma}})^{2}}-e^{-\frac{1}{2}s^{2}}\right|ds

\displaystyle=-\int_{|s|\leqslant n^{\gamma/2}}\left|e^{-\frac{1}{2}(s+\frac{1}{\sigma n^{\gamma}})^{2}}-e^{-\frac{1}{2}s^{2}}\right|ds-\int_{|s|>n^{\gamma/2}}\left|e^{-\frac{1}{2}(s+\frac{1}{\sigma n^{\gamma}})^{2}}-e^{-\frac{1}{2}s^{2}}\right|ds

\displaystyle=:J_{11}+J_{12}.

For
J_{11}
, using again the inequality
e^{x}-1\leqslant|x|+|x|e^{x}
,
x\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

\displaystyle J_{11}

\displaystyle=-\int_{|s|\leqslant n^{\gamma/2}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}s^{2}}\left|e^{-\frac{s}{n^{\gamma}}-\frac{1}{2n^{2\gamma}}}-1\right|ds

\displaystyle\geqslant-\int_{|s|\leqslant n^{\gamma/2}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}s^{2}}\left|\frac{s}{n^{\gamma}}+\frac{1}{n^{2\gamma}}\right|ds-\int_{|s|\leqslant n^{\gamma/2}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}(s+\frac{1}{n^{\gamma}})^{2}}\left|\frac{s}{n^{\gamma}}+\frac{1}{2n^{2\gamma}}\right|ds

\displaystyle\geqslant-2\int_{|s|\leqslant 2n^{\gamma/2}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}s^{2}}\left|\frac{s}{n^{\gamma}}+\frac{1}{n^{2\gamma}}\right|ds

\displaystyle\geqslant-\frac{2}{n^{\gamma}}\int_{|s|\leqslant 2n^{\gamma/2}}|s|e^{-\frac{1}{2}s^{2}}ds-\frac{2}{n^{2\gamma}}\int_{|s|\leqslant 2n^{\gamma/2}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}s^{2}}ds\geqslant-\frac{c}{n^{\gamma}}.

For
J_{12}
, we have

\displaystyle I_{12}\leqslant\int_{n^{\gamma/2}}^{n^{\gamma/2}+\frac{1}{n^{\gamma}}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}s^{2}}ds+\int_{-n^{\gamma/2}}^{-n^{\gamma/2}+\frac{1}{n^{\gamma}}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}s^{2}}ds\leqslant\frac{2}{n^{\gamma}}.

Hence, we get
I_{1}\leqslant\frac{c}{n^{\gamma}}.
Similarly, one can also check that
I_{2}\leqslant\frac{c}{n^{\gamma}}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\int_{t}^{\infty}\psi\left(s,\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}-\frac{1}{\sigma n^{\gamma}}\right)ds-\int_{t}^{\infty}\psi\left(s,\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)ds\geqslant-\frac{c}{n^{\gamma}}.

Using (
7.42
) and taking
\gamma=\frac{\delta}{2(3+\delta)}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{x+S_{n}}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}>t,\tau_{x}>n\right)

\displaystyle\geqslant\int_{t}^{\infty}\psi\left(s,\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)ds-\frac{c}{n^{\gamma}}-\frac{c_{\gamma}}{n^{\frac{\delta}{2}-\gamma(2+\delta)}}

(7.47)

\displaystyle\geqslant\int_{t}^{\infty}\psi\left(s,\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)ds-\frac{c_{\gamma}}{n^{\frac{\delta}{2(3+\delta)}}}.

Combining (
7.2
) and (
7.2
) finishes the proof of (
2.24
).
∎

Proof of Corollary
2.9
.

Note that

(7.48)

\displaystyle I:=\int_{0}^{t}\psi\left(s,\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)ds=e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}n}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{s^{2}}{2}}e^{\frac{sx}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}}\left(1-e^{-\frac{2sx}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}}\right)ds.

For the upper bound, using the inequality
1-e^{-u}\leqslant u
,
u\geqslant 0
, we get that for any
x\geqslant 0
and
t\geqslant 0
,

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{2x}{\sigma\sqrt{2\pi n}}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}n}}\int_{0}^{t}se^{-\frac{s^{2}}{2}}e^{\frac{sx}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}}ds

\displaystyle=\frac{2x}{\sigma\sqrt{2\pi n}}\int_{0}^{t}se^{-\frac{1}{2}(s-\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}})^{2}}ds=\frac{2x}{\sigma\sqrt{2\pi n}}\int_{-\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}}^{t-\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}}\left(u+\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\right)e^{-\frac{1}{2}u^{2}}du

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{2x}{\sigma\sqrt{2\pi n}}\left(e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}n}}-e^{-\frac{1}{2}(t-\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}})^{2}}\right)+c\frac{x^{2}}{n}

\displaystyle=\frac{2x}{\sigma\sqrt{2\pi n}}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}n}}\left(1-e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{2}+\frac{tx}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}}\right)+c\frac{x^{2}}{n}

(7.49)

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{2V(x)}{\sigma\sqrt{2\pi n}}\left(1-e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{2}}\right)+c\frac{x^{2}}{n}=\frac{2V(x)}{\sigma\sqrt{2\pi n}}\Phi^{+}(t)+c\frac{x^{2}}{n},

where in the last inequality we used the fact that
e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}n}}\leqslant 1
,
t\geqslant 0
and
x\leqslant V(x)
.

For the lower bound,
from (
7.48
)
and the inequalities
e^{\frac{sx}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}}\geqslant 1
and
1-e^{-u}\geqslant u-\frac{u^{2}}{2}
,
u\geqslant 0
,
we obtain that for any
x\geqslant 0
and
t\geqslant 0
,

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle\geqslant e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}n}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{s^{2}}{2}}\left(1-e^{-\frac{2sx}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}}\right)ds

\displaystyle\geqslant e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}n}}\frac{2x}{\sigma\sqrt{2\pi n}}\left(\int_{0}^{t}se^{-\frac{s^{2}}{2}}ds-\frac{x}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\int_{0}^{t}s^{2}e^{-\frac{s^{2}}{2}}ds\right)

\displaystyle\geqslant\left(1-c\frac{x^{2}}{n}\right)\frac{2x}{\sigma\sqrt{2\pi n}}\left(\Phi^{+}(t)-c_{t}\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\right)

(7.50)

\displaystyle\geqslant\frac{2x}{\sigma\sqrt{2\pi n}}\left(\Phi^{+}(t)-c_{t}\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\right),

where
c_{t}=\frac{1}{\sigma}\int_{0}^{t}s^{2}e^{-\frac{s^{2}}{2}}ds
.
Applying Lemma
3.7
with
k_{0}=n
, we get that uniformly in
x\in[n^{1/2-\varepsilon},\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}]
,

\displaystyle x\geqslant\frac{1}{1+c_{\varepsilon}k_{0}^{-\varepsilon}}\left(V(x)-c_{\varepsilon}k_{0}^{1/2-\varepsilon}\right)

\displaystyle\geqslant V(x)-c_{\varepsilon}\frac{V(x)}{n^{\varepsilon}}-c_{\varepsilon}n^{1/2-\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\geqslant V(x)\left(1-\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon}}\right).

Substituting this into (
7.2
) gives that uniformly in
x\in[n^{1/2-\varepsilon},\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}]
,

(7.51)

\displaystyle I\geqslant\frac{2V(x)}{\sigma\sqrt{2\pi n}}\left(1-\frac{c_{\varepsilon}}{n^{\varepsilon}}\right)\left(\Phi^{+}(t)-c_{t}\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\geqslant\frac{2V(x)}{\sigma\sqrt{2\pi n}}\left(\Phi^{+}(t)-c_{t}^{\prime}\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\right),

where
c_{t}^{\prime}
is bounded uniformly in
t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
.
Combining (
7.2
) and (
7.51
),
and taking into account Theorems
2.7
and
2.8
, we conclude the proof of (
2.26
).

For the second assertion (
2.27
),
applying Lemma
3.7
with
k_{0}=n^{\beta}
,
we get that for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1/2)
and
\beta\in(0,1/2-\varepsilon)
, uniformly in
x\in[n^{\beta},\alpha_{n}\sqrt{n}]
,

\displaystyle x\geqslant\frac{1}{1+c_{\varepsilon}k_{0}^{-\varepsilon}}\left(V(x)-c_{\varepsilon}k_{0}^{1/2-\varepsilon}\right)

\displaystyle\geqslant V(x)-c_{\varepsilon}n^{-\varepsilon\beta}V(x)-c_{\varepsilon}n^{\beta/2}

\displaystyle\geqslant V(x)-c_{\varepsilon}n^{-\varepsilon\beta}V(x)-c_{\varepsilon}xn^{-\beta/2}

\displaystyle\geqslant V(x)\left(1-c_{\varepsilon}n^{-\varepsilon\beta}\right).

Substituting this into (
7.2
), we get that uniformly in
x\in[n^{\beta},n^{1/2-\varepsilon}]
,

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle\geqslant\frac{2V(x)}{\sigma\sqrt{2\pi n}}\left(1-c_{\varepsilon}n^{-\varepsilon\beta}\right)\left(\Phi^{+}(t)-c_{t}\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\right)

\displaystyle\geqslant\frac{2V(x)}{\sigma\sqrt{2\pi n}}\left(\Phi^{+}(t)-c_{\varepsilon}n^{-\varepsilon\beta}\right).

This, together with (
7.2
) and Theorems
2.7
and
2.8
,
finishes the proof of (
2.27
).
∎
